### Task 3: Retire the `.p-*` dialect

Thirty classes, roughly 180 emission sites, eleven source files, five test assertions. The classes are aliases beside an unprefixed twin, so this is a rename at the call sites and a deletion in the CSS. It is its own task because the styles rule cannot tighten until it is done, and nothing else in the suite catches a half-finished pass.

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/src/panels/account.ts`, `panels/config.ts`, `panels/connect.ts`, `panels/connectCard.ts`, `web/src/plugins/builtin/characters.ts`, `builtin/lootTracker.ts`, `builtin/xpTracker.ts`, `builtin/notes.ts`, `builtin/screenshot.ts`, `web/src/plugins/pluginsPanel.ts`, `web/src/plugins/settingsForm.ts`, `web/styleguide.html`
- Modify (four emitters the "eleven prefixed emitters" count misses; re-verified by `grep -rn "\bp-[a-z]" web/src` at HEAD): `web/src/plugins/builtin/bank.ts:40` (`p-btn p-btn-primary`), `builtin/tasks.ts:90,168` (`p-input snippet-code`, the snippet box and the inline task editor), `builtin/tasksViews.ts:225,227,228` (`p-input` on the select, number and text param controls), `builtin/traceView.ts:135` (`p-input` on the kind select)
- Modify (the one `.p-` rule in the stylesheet layer, parked in `legacy.css` by Task 2 Step 3): `.trace-head .p-input` becomes `.trace-head .input` and returns to `web/src/styles/layout/trace.css`
- Modify: `web/src/styles/families.ts`, `styles.test.ts`, `card.css`, `alert.css`, `form.css`, `layout/panels.css`
- Delete: `web/src/styles/legacy.css`
- Test: `web/src/panels/connect.test.ts:39,73,156`, `web/src/plugins/builtin/characters.test.ts:189`, `web/src/plugins/builtin/screenshot.test.ts:37`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the family files from Task 2.
- Produces: no `.p-` class anywhere in `web/`. The mapping every later task assumes:

| old | new | old | new |

| `p-btn` | `btn` | `p-msg` | `alert` |
| `p-btn-primary` | `btn-primary` | `p-muted` | `muted` |
| `p-btn-danger` | `btn-danger` | `p-error` | `alert alert-error` |

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing assertion that no source file emits a `.p-` class**

Add to `web/src/styles/styles.test.ts`:

```ts
it('no source file emits a .p- class', () => {
  const offenders: string[] = [];
  const walk = (dir: string): void => {
    for (const entry of readdirSync(join(here, '..', dir), { withFileTypes: true })) {
      const rel = `${dir}/${entry.name}`;
      if (entry.isDirectory()) { walk(rel); continue; }
      if (!entry.name.endsWith('.ts') || entry.name.endsWith('.test.ts')) continue;
      const src = readFileSync(join(here, '..', rel), 'utf8');
      // Anchored on the class attribute, never on a bare "p-": script ids like chop-and-drop
      // match that and there are 81 of them.
      for (const m of src.matchAll(/class(?:Name)?\s*[:=]\s*[`'"]([^`'"]*)/g)) {
        if (/\bp-[a-z]/.test(m[1])) offenders.push(`${rel}: ${m[1]}`);
      }
      for (const m of src.matchAll(/class="([^"]*)"/g)) {
        if (/\bp-[a-z]/.test(m[1])) offenders.push(`${rel}: ${m[1]}`);
      }
    }
  };
  for (const root of ['ui', 'panels', 'plugins', 'frame', 'bank', 'characters', 'home', 'stats']) walk(root);
  expect(offenders).toEqual([]);
});
```

Run: `cd web && npx vitest run src/styles/styles.test.ts` - expect FAIL, listing roughly 180 offenders across eleven files.

- [ ] **Step 2: Convert the eleven files, one at a time**

Work down the Files list, applying the mapping above. Each file is `innerHTML` template strings (except `settingsForm.ts`, which uses `document.createElement`), so the change is textual. Run the offenders case after each file and watch the list shrink; that is the progress meter.

Three groups have no unprefixed twin and need their rule moved, not just renamed: `p-notes` (the notes textarea's fill behaviour), `p-shot-strip` / `p-shot-thumb` (the screenshot grid) and the eight `p-plugin-*` row classes. Move each into `layout/panels.css` under the new name; they are per-surface geometry, which is what a layout file is for. `p-settings-form` and `p-setting` follow the same route; `p-setting-label` becomes the existing `.field-label` in `form.css`.

`p-error` becomes `alert alert-error`. Keep the ids untouched (`#attach-error`, `#char-panel-error`, `#account-error`): `setAccountError` (`panels/account.ts:57-59`) and the characters panel's `setError` (`characters.ts:43-46`) both address them by id, and both toggle `.hidden`, which still works on an alert.

`p-ok` has no caller anywhere in the repository, but it does not have a rule of its own: `forms.css:70` is `.notice, .p-ok { ... }` and `:72` is `.p-error, .p-ok { ... }`, and `.notice` does have callers. Drop the `.p-ok` **selector** from both rules: keep `.notice` at `:70` with its declarations, and fold `:72`'s `.p-error` declarations into the `alert alert-error` conversion above rather than deleting them with the line.

### Task 4: The core family (button, badge, tag, dot, alert, section label) and the copy module

The first of four family tasks. Each lands its CSS, its builder where the component is stateful enough to want one, and **its styleguide section in the same commit**: a component that is not on the styleguide does not exist, and adding a family without its styleguide entry is an incomplete change (companion spec section 5, part 2).

This task also creates `web/src/ui/copy.ts`, the single home for every mock string that carries an em dash (ruling R1). It ships with all twelve, eight constants and four templates, even though most of their consumers arrive later, because the point of the hedge is that a reversal is one file.

Design source: `map-design.md` sections 2.1 to 2.6, and note S7 (the mock's borders are `.06`/`.07`/`.09`, not the component files' `.05`/`.12`) and S17 (ruling R17's badge sizes).

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `copy.ts`**

```ts
// web/src/ui/copy.ts -- UI copy quoted verbatim from the shell v2 mock.
//
// Plan ruling R1: the sprint constraint forbids em dashes in NEW prose; these strings are design
// literals, copied from docs/design/idlescape-shell-v2/Idlescape Shell v2.dc.html, which the
// authority order puts above every other document for literals. They live here, together, so a
// reversal of that ruling is one file and one commit rather than a hunt through eleven surfaces.
// Every consumer imports from here; no surface inlines one of these strings.

export const BAR_UNPAIRED = 'not paired — Claude can play this character alongside you';
export const BAR_PAUSED = 'paused — you took control';
export const LOOT_EMPTY_COPY = 'Items you pick up appear here as you play — or as Claude plays for you.';
export const EVENTS_EMPTY_COPY = 'Loosen the filters — or go make something happen.';
export const EVENT_PAUSED_MOUSE = 'Paused — you moved the mouse';
export const EVENT_GATEWAY_OK = 'Gateway connected — ws ok';
export const BANK_FOOTER_NOTE = 'view & reorder only — items move in game';
export const CANVAS_CAPTION_NOTE = '(same-origin iframe — never restyled)';

/** The run card's paused status line. */
export const pausedResumes = (seconds: number): string =>
  `You took control — resumes in ${seconds}s. Esc pauses again any time.`;
/** An Events row for a run that failed with a reason. */
export const runFailed = (script: string, reason: string): string => `${script} failed — ${reason}`;
/** The trace window footer. `.dc.html:134`: middot separator, em dash inside the clause. */
export const tracePoppedOut = (count: number): string => `${count} events · popped out — the panel stays free`;
/**
 * An Events row for a bank version bump. The mock writes `Bank updated — 27 / 240 slots used`
 * with fixed numbers; the producer (Task 11) formats the same sentence with real ones, so the
 * sentence lives here as a template and never as a const with the mock's 27 in it.
 */
export const bankUpdated = (used: number, total: number): string =>
  `Bank updated — ${used} / ${total} slots used`;

/** Every plain-string export above, for copy.test.ts. Keep in declaration order. */
export const EM_DASH_COPY: readonly string[] = [
  BAR_UNPAIRED, BAR_PAUSED, LOOT_EMPTY_COPY, EVENTS_EMPTY_COPY, EVENT_PAUSED_MOUSE,
  EVENT_GATEWAY_OK, BANK_FOOTER_NOTE, CANVAS_CAPTION_NOTE
];
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `parts.ts`**

```ts
// web/src/ui/parts.ts -- the shell v2 component library's stateful half, as h() builders.
//
// One builder per component that carries variants or state; a component that is only a class
// (Tag, SectionLabel) still gets one so no surface hand-writes the class name. Every class here
// is defined in exactly one family stylesheet; see web/src/styles/families.ts.
import { h } from './el';

export type DotTone = 'idle' | 'ok' | 'accent' | 'warn' | 'error';

/** Uppercase 9.5px group heading. `docs/design/idlescape-shell-v2/components/core/SectionLabel.jsx`. */
export function sectionLabel(text: string): HTMLElement {
  return h('span', { class: 'section-label' }, text);
}

/** A quiet noun. Not a status: use `badge()` from el.ts for state. `core/Tag.jsx`. */
export function tag(text: string): HTMLElement {
  return h('span', { class: 'tag' }, text);
}

/**
 * The status dot. `size` is the mock's per-site override (9 in the co-pilot bar, 8 in the Claude
 * session rows, 7 on a character tab, 6 in the strip corner); the class carries the 8px default,
 * so an unsized dot has no inline style at all.
 */
export function dot(tone: DotTone = 'idle', opts: { pulse?: boolean; glow?: boolean; size?: number; explicit?: boolean } = {}): HTMLElement {
  const classes = ['dot'];
  // `idle` is the default and takes the family's own --text-faint grey. `explicit` opts into the
  // mock's lighter #666, which only the co-pilot bar's unpaired dot uses (map-design 3.3).
  if (tone !== 'idle' || opts.explicit) classes.push(`dot-${tone}`);
  if (opts.pulse) classes.push('dot-pulse');
  if (opts.glow) classes.push('dot-glow');
  const el = h('span', { class: classes.join(' '), 'aria-hidden': 'true' });
  if (opts.size !== undefined) { el.style.width = `${opts.size}px`; el.style.height = `${opts.size}px`; }
  return el;
}
```

Run: `cd web && npx vitest run src/ui/parts.test.ts` - expect PASS.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write `button.css`**

Replace the file. Sizes and variants from `map-design.md` section 2.1; the mock's borders win over `Button.jsx` per note S7 (`rgba(255,255,255,.09)` on default, `.12` reserved for outline).

```css
/* Button. docs/design/idlescape-shell-v2/components/core/Button.{jsx,d.ts,prompt.md}.
   Four sizes (xs 22 / sm 25 / md 27 / lg 29) and five variants. Never two primaries side by
   side; xs is for row-level actions, lg for panel-width CTAs. The .13s transition on background,
   border-color, color, box-shadow, transform and opacity comes from base.css. */
.btn {
  display: inline-flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; gap: 6px;
  height: var(--ctl-h); padding: 0 13px;
  background: var(--control); border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,.09);
  border-radius: var(--r-ctl); color: var(--text-strong);
  font-family: var(--font-ui); font-size: var(--fs-base); font-weight: 500;
  white-space: nowrap; cursor: pointer;
}
.btn:hover:not(:disabled) { background: var(--control-hover); transform: translateY(-1px); }
.btn:disabled { opacity: .35; cursor: not-allowed; transform: none; }

.btn-primary {
  background: var(--accent-grad); border-color: var(--accent-deep);
  color: var(--on-accent); font-weight: 650;
  box-shadow: 0 2px 10px var(--accent-glow);
}
.btn-primary:hover:not(:disabled) { background: var(--accent-grad); box-shadow: 0 3px 16px rgba(255,152,31,.45); transform: translateY(-1px); }
/* Two glow contexts, from map-design section 4 lines 267-268. The bare .btn-primary above is
   the co-pilot bar's, the only one the mock draws at .28 resting.
     .btn-in-card  a primary inside a card (Run, Run now): rests .25, hovers .35.
     .btn-cta      a panel-width call to action (Open bank window at h29, Capture the canvas at
                   h29, the loot empty state's Run a script at h27): rests .25, keeps the base
                   .45 hover.
   Both are modifiers on .btn and not a `.card .btn-primary` descendant selector, because
   styles.test.ts takes classes[0] of a selector as its subject and button.css does not own
   `card`; the card's own caller adds the class. Sizing does not decide this: the loot CTA is
   md and the run card's Run is sm, so a size-keyed rule would get both wrong. */
.btn-primary.btn-in-card { box-shadow: 0 2px 10px rgba(255,152,31,.25); }
.btn-primary.btn-in-card:hover:not(:disabled) { box-shadow: 0 2px 12px rgba(255,152,31,.35); }
.btn-primary.btn-cta { box-shadow: 0 2px 10px rgba(255,152,31,.25); }

.btn-danger { background: var(--danger); border-color: var(--danger-deep); color: #f0e6e6; }
.btn-danger:hover:not(:disabled) { background: var(--danger-hover); transform: translateY(-1px); }

.btn-outline { background: none; border-color: var(--hairline-strong); color: var(--text-quiet); }
.btn-outline:hover:not(:disabled) { background: var(--control); color: var(--text-strong); transform: none; }

.btn-link { height: auto; padding: 0; background: none; border: 0; color: var(--accent); box-shadow: none; }
.btn-link:hover:not(:disabled) { background: none; text-decoration: underline; transform: none; }

/* Three icon-button geometries, all drawn by the mock and all different: the two window closes
   at 24/14 (map-design 3.5 and 3.6) are the base, the panel-header close is 26/15 (3.7) and the
   pinned-tracker unpin is 18/12 with a 4px radius (3.8). One family, two modifiers; collapsing
   them into one would shrink the panel close by 2px and cost it a point of type. */
.btn-icon { width: var(--ctl-h-tiny); height: var(--ctl-h-tiny); padding: 0; background: none; border: 0; color: var(--text-muted); font-size: 14px; }
.btn-icon:hover:not(:disabled) { background: var(--control); color: var(--text-strong); transform: none; }
.btn-icon-lg { width: 26px; height: 26px; font-size: 15px; }
.btn-icon-xs { width: 18px; height: 18px; border-radius: 4px; font-size: 12px; }

.btn-xs { height: var(--ctl-h-xs); padding: 0 9px; font-size: var(--fs-tiny); }
.btn-sm { height: var(--ctl-h-sm); padding: 0 11px; font-size: var(--fs-small); }
.btn-lg { height: var(--ctl-h-lg); padding: 0 14px; font-size: var(--fs-base); }
.btn-quiet { height: var(--ctl-h-quiet); padding: 0 9px; font-size: var(--fs-small); }
.btn-block { display: flex; width: 100%; }
.btn-row { display: flex; gap: 6px; }
.link { background: none; border: 0; padding: 0; color: var(--accent); font-size: inherit; cursor: pointer; }
.link:hover { text-decoration: underline; }
```

Task 8 puts `.btn-icon-lg` on `#panel-back` and Task 19 puts `.btn-icon-xs` on `[data-unpin]`; the bare `.btn-icon` is the trace and bank window closes.

`.btn-quiet` is the home for the mock's 23px and 24px controls (the panel-header Pin, the XP Reset, the Claude Copy, the bank footer buttons); note S22 records that 23 and 24 are not declared control heights, and this is where they live rather than being inlined at five call sites.

- [ ] **Step 7: Write `badge.css`**

```css
/* Badge, Tag and StatusDot. components/core/{Badge,Tag,StatusDot}.jsx.
   Four badge geometries ship as one family with three modifiers (plan ruling R17). */
.badge {
  display: inline-flex; align-items: center; gap: 4px;
  height: 17px; padding: 0 7px; border-radius: 9px;
  background: var(--control); color: var(--text-quiet);
  font-size: var(--fs-micro); font-weight: 650; white-space: nowrap;
}
.badge-accent { background: rgba(255,152,31,.16); color: var(--accent); }
.badge-ok     { background: rgba(67,160,71,.16);  color: var(--ok-bright); }
.badge-warn   { background: rgba(255,179,0,.14);  color: var(--warn); }
.badge-error  { background: rgba(229,57,53,.16);  color: var(--error-text); }
.badge-info   { background: rgba(74,144,217,.16); color: var(--info-bright); }
/* The run card's state badge. */
.badge-lg { height: 19px; padding: 0 8px; border-radius: 10px; font-weight: 700; letter-spacing: .03em; }
/* Script-card badges: Tag geometry, Badge colours. */
.badge-sm { height: 16px; padding: 0 7px; border-radius: 8px; }
/* The Events row's character badge. */
.badge-quiet { height: 15px; padding: 0 5px; border-radius: 8px; background: var(--track); color: var(--text-muted); font-size: 9px; font-weight: 500; }
.badge-dot { width: 5px; height: 5px; border-radius: 50%; background: currentColor; flex: none; }

.tag {
  display: inline-flex; align-items: center;
  height: 16px; padding: 0 7px; border-radius: 8px;
  background: var(--control); color: var(--text-quiet);
  font-size: var(--fs-micro); white-space: nowrap;
}

/* Two idle greys, both drawn by the mock and both needed: the default --text-faint (#575755) is
   the char-tab offline dot and the Claude "work desktop" session row, and --dot-idle (#666) is
   the co-pilot bar's unpaired dot (map-design 3.3). `dot()` emits .dot-idle only when the caller
   passes `{ explicit: true }`, so the token added for it is not dead. */
.dot { display: inline-block; flex: none; width: 8px; height: 8px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--text-faint); }
.dot-idle   { background: var(--dot-idle); }
.dot-ok     { background: var(--ok); }
.dot-accent { background: var(--accent); }
.dot-warn   { background: var(--warn); }
.dot-error  { background: var(--error); }
.dot-glow.dot-ok     { box-shadow: 0 0 8px rgba(67,160,71,.6); }
.dot-glow.dot-accent { box-shadow: 0 0 8px rgba(255,152,31,.6); }
.dot-glow.dot-warn   { box-shadow: 0 0 8px rgba(255,179,0,.5); }
.dot-glow.dot-error  { box-shadow: 0 0 8px rgba(229,57,53,.5); }
.dot-pulse { animation: pulse 1.6s ease-in-out infinite; }
```

**Retire the six shipped `.dot-*` aliases in this step, not later.** `data.css:39-43` defines `.dot-green, .dot-ok`, `.dot-grey, .dot-idle`, `.dot-red, .dot-error`, `.dot-amber, .dot-warn` and `.dot-live`; Task 2 moved all of them into `badge.css` and this step replaces the file. Two files emit the old names and both break silently if they are not converted here:

```
web/src/frame/runBanner.ts:154   'strip-dot dot dot-accent dot-live'  -> 'strip-dot dot dot-accent dot-pulse'
web/src/panels/connect.ts:156    'dot dot-green dot-live'             -> 'dot dot-ok dot-glow dot-pulse'
web/src/panels/connect.ts:156    'dot dot-grey'                       -> 'dot'
web/src/styles/strip.css         '.strip-dot.dot-live'                -> '.strip-dot.dot-pulse'
```

`runBanner.ts` dies in Task 14 and `connect.ts` is rebuilt in Task 20, but both render in every commit in between, and a strip dot that stopped pulsing is not something the suite would tell you about. Re-grep `dot-green|dot-grey|dot-idle|dot-red|dot-amber|dot-live` across `web/src` before moving on; the list above was taken at `1bfdc7d` and must be empty afterwards.

Into `alert.css`, replacing the moved `.alert*` rules (`map-design.md` section 2.5). Keep `.empty` as it is; Task 19 adds `.empty-state`.

```css
/* Alert. components/core/Alert.jsx: a 3px tone rail over a 7% tint of the same tone. */
```

### Task 5: The forms family (input, select, textarea, checkbox, segmented)

Design source: `map-design.md` sections 2.8 to 2.12. The mock draws inputs at five heights (23, 24, 25, 27, 29); the component's height is 27 and the rest are size modifiers, not new components.

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/src/styles/form.css`, `web/src/styles/tab.css` (the `.seg*` and `.chip*` block; `families.ts` puts those two prefixes there, not in `form.css`), `web/styleguide.html`
- Modify: `web/src/ui/parts.ts`, `web/src/ui/parts.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `h` from `el.ts`; `parts.ts` from Task 4.
- Produces:
  - `export interface SegmentedOption { value: string; label: string }`
  - `export function segmented(options: readonly SegmentedOption[], value: string, onChange: (value: string) => void): HTMLElement`
  - `export function chip(label: string, active: boolean, onToggle: () => void): HTMLButtonElement`
  - CSS in `form.css`: `.input`, `.input-sm`, `.input-quiet`, `.input-lg`, `.input.mono`, `.select`, `.select-sm`, `.textarea`, `.textarea.code`, `.switch`, `.field`, `.field-inline`, `.field-label`.
  - CSS in `tab.css`: `.seg`, `.seg-btn`, `.seg-btn.active`, `.chip`, `.chip.active`. They are forms components by behaviour and `tab.css` classes by ownership; `families.ts` assigns `seg` and `chip` to `tab.css`, and putting them in `form.css` fails `form.css defines only its own family` seven times over. Their styleguide demos stay in `#forms`, where a reader expects them; the completeness rule scans the whole page and does not care which section a class is demonstrated in.

```ts
import { chip, segmented } from './parts';

describe('segmented', () => {
  const OPTIONS = [{ value: 'my', label: 'My tasks' }, { value: 'market', label: 'Marketplace' }];

  it('renders one button per option and marks exactly the selected one', () => {
    const el = segmented(OPTIONS, 'market', () => {});
    const btns = [...el.querySelectorAll('button')];
    expect(btns.map(b => b.textContent)).toEqual(['My tasks', 'Marketplace']);
    expect(btns.map(b => b.classList.contains('active'))).toEqual([false, true]);
    // Mutation target: dropping aria-pressed leaves a control with no announced state.
    expect(btns.map(b => b.getAttribute('aria-pressed'))).toEqual(['false', 'true']);
  });

  it('reports the clicked value and does not repaint itself', () => {
    const seen: string[] = [];
    const el = segmented(OPTIONS, 'my', v => seen.push(v));
    el.querySelectorAll('button')[1].click();
    expect(seen).toEqual(['market']);
    // The owner re-renders; the control does not guess. Its own classes are unchanged.
    expect(el.querySelectorAll('button')[1].classList.contains('active')).toBe(false);
  });

  it('does not fire for a click on the option that is already selected', () => {
    const seen: string[] = [];
    const el = segmented(OPTIONS, 'my', v => seen.push(v));
    el.querySelectorAll('button')[0].click();
    expect(seen).toEqual([]);
  });
});

describe('chip', () => {
  it('is a toggle with aria-pressed mirroring the active class', () => {
    const on = chip('XP', true, () => {});
    expect(on.className).toBe('chip active');
    expect(on.getAttribute('aria-pressed')).toBe('true');
    expect(chip('Loot', false, () => {}).className).toBe('chip');
  });
  it('fires on every click, including one that turns it off', () => {
    let n = 0;
    const el = chip('XP', true, () => { n++; });
    el.click(); el.click();
    expect(n).toBe(2);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Implement both builders**

Append to `web/src/ui/parts.ts`:

```ts
export interface SegmentedOption { value: string; label: string }

/**
 * The segmented control. `components/forms/Segmented.jsx`, signature from README's component
 * table. It reports and does not repaint: the owner re-renders with the new value, exactly as
 * every panel in this shell already does, so there is never a moment when the control's idea of
 * the selection and the panel's disagree. Clicking the active option is a no-op.
 */
export function segmented(options: readonly SegmentedOption[], value: string, onChange: (value: string) => void): HTMLElement {
  return h('div', { class: 'seg', role: 'group' },
    ...options.map(o => h('button', {
      type: 'button',
      class: o.value === value ? 'seg-btn active' : 'seg-btn',
      'aria-pressed': o.value === value ? 'true' : 'false',
      'data-seg': o.value,
      onclick: () => { if (o.value !== value) onChange(o.value); }
    }, o.label))
  );
}

/** A multi-select filter chip. Unlike `segmented`, every click fires: it is its own toggle. */
export function chip(label: string, active: boolean, onToggle: () => void): HTMLButtonElement {
  return h('button', {
    type: 'button',
    class: active ? 'chip active' : 'chip',
    'aria-pressed': active ? 'true' : 'false',
    onclick: onToggle
  }, label);
}
```

Run: `cd web && npx vitest run src/ui/parts.test.ts` - expect PASS.

- [ ] **Step 2: Implement both builders**

Append to `web/src/ui/parts.ts` (and add `Child` to the `el.ts` import):

```ts
export type Rail = 'accent' | 'ok' | 'warn' | 'error';

/** `components/data/Card.jsx`. `hero` is the gradient plus drop shadow the run card uses. */
export function card(opts: { rail?: Rail; hero?: boolean; class?: string } = {}, ...children: Child[]): HTMLElement {
  const classes = ['card'];
  if (opts.hero) classes.push('card-hero');
  if (opts.rail) classes.push(`card-rail-${opts.rail}`);
  if (opts.class) classes.push(opts.class);
  return h('div', { class: classes.join(' ') }, ...children);
}

/**
 * `components/data/Meter.jsx`. Purely decorative: every meter in this shell sits beside the same
 * number written out in text, so the track carries aria-hidden rather than a redundant
 * progressbar role that a screen reader would read twice.
 */
export function meter(pct: number, opts: { tone?: 'accent' | 'hp' | 'prayer' | 'run'; shimmer?: boolean } = {}): HTMLElement {
  const classes = ['meter-fill'];
  if (opts.tone && opts.tone !== 'accent') classes.push(`meter-${opts.tone}`);
  if (opts.shimmer) classes.push('shimmer');
  const fill = h('span', { class: classes.join(' ') });
  fill.style.width = `${Math.min(100, Math.max(0, pct))}%`;
  return h('div', { class: 'meter', 'aria-hidden': 'true' }, fill);
}
```

Into `card.css`, beside the section label from Task 4:

The name `.card` is free because Task 2 Step 0 renamed the gate, home and character-creation screen card to `.screen-card`. If `grep -rn 'class="card"' web/src` finds anything outside a panel, that rename did not land and this rule will silently restyle three full-screen surfaces no test covers. Check before writing it.

```css
/* Card. components/data/Card.jsx, with the mock's own border alphas (map-design S7): .06 for a
   panel card, .07 for the two hero cards, never the component file's .05. */
.card { padding: 11px; background: var(--card); border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,.06); border-radius: var(--r-card); }
.card-hero { padding: 12px; background: var(--card-grad); border-color: var(--hairline); box-shadow: 0 4px 14px -8px rgba(0,0,0,.6); }
.card-rail-accent { border-left: 3px solid var(--accent); }
.card-rail-ok     { border-left: 3px solid var(--ok); }
.card-rail-warn   { border-left: 3px solid var(--warn); }
.card-rail-error  { border-left: 3px solid var(--error); }
.card-lift { transition: transform var(--dur) var(--ease), border-color var(--dur) var(--ease); }
.card-lift:hover { transform: translateY(-1px); border-color: var(--hairline-strong); }

/* KV. components/data/KV.jsx. */
```

```ts
export function kv(label: string, value: Child, opts: { id?: string; num?: boolean; roomy?: boolean; tone?: string } = {}): HTMLElement {
  const val = h('span', { class: opts.num ? 'kv-value num' : 'kv-value', id: opts.id }, value);
  if (opts.tone) val.style.color = opts.tone;
  return h('div', { class: opts.roomy ? 'kv kv-roomy' : 'kv' }, h('span', { class: 'kv-label' }, label), val);
}
```

### Task 7: The overlay family (pills, HUD, the XP drop, window chrome, the menu)

Everything that floats speaks one language: a black scrim, a 1px tone-tinted border, a 3px radius, bold mono and a 2px backdrop blur. This task writes the classes and the styleguide section; Task 13 wires the pills to real data and Tasks 16 and 17 use the window chrome.

**This task owns the `Window` family for the whole project (decision D22), so it writes a stated default and a stated modifier rather than transcribing one of the two windows.** The Script Studio (sprint entry 6) composes this family and does not extract its own; `2026-09-07-script-studio-design.md` section 4.2 is the specification for the split, and its `.win` / `.win-bank` names are that document's superseded proposal. The shipping names are the ones below.

Design source: `map-design.md` sections 2.16, 2.17, 3.4, 3.5 and 3.6, plus note S20 (the two canvas corner pills are `padding:3px 8px; font-size:11px`, where `OverlayPill.jsx` is `2px 7px` at 10.5; both ship), and `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-07-script-studio-design.md` section 4.2 for the window table.

- Modify: `web/src/ui/parts.ts`, `web/src/ui/parts.test.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/styles/families.ts` (add `menu` to `overlay.css`'s prefix list)

- [ ] **Step 2: Implement them**

```ts
export type PillTone = 'accent' | 'ok' | 'warn' | 'error' | 'neutral';

/**
 * The only language spoken over the game canvas (`components/overlay/OverlayPill.prompt.md`).
 * `corner` is the larger geometry the mock uses for the two canvas corner pills (3px 8px at 11px)
 * against the component's own 2px 7px at 10.5px; both ship, per map-design note S20.
 */
export function pill(text: string, tone: PillTone = 'accent', opts: { corner?: boolean } = {}): HTMLElement {
  const classes = ['ov-pill', `ov-pill-${tone}`];
  if (opts.corner) classes.push('ov-pill-corner');
  return h('span', { class: classes.join(' ') }, text);
}

const HUD_LABEL = { hp: 'HP', prayer: 'PRAY', run: 'RUN' } as const;

/** `components/overlay/HudPill.jsx`: an OverlayPill with a 42x4 bar between label and value. */
export function hudPill(meterName: 'hp' | 'prayer' | 'run', pct: number, value: string): HTMLElement {
  const label = HUD_LABEL[meterName];
  const fill = h('span', { class: `hud-fill hud-fill-${meterName}` });
  fill.style.width = `${Math.min(100, Math.max(0, pct))}%`;
  return h('span', { class: `ov-pill hud-pill hud-pill-${meterName}`, 'aria-label': `${label} ${value}` },
    h('span', { class: 'hud-label' }, label),
    h('span', { class: 'hud-bar', 'aria-hidden': 'true' }, fill),
    h('span', { class: 'hud-value' }, value)
  );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `overlay.css`**

```css
/* Everything that floats over the game canvas, and the chrome of the two windows that float
   over the stage. components/overlay/{OverlayPill,HudPill}.jsx and the mock's window frames.
   Transparency and blur live here and nowhere else: never inside a panel. */
.ov-pill {
  display: inline-flex; align-items: center; gap: 6px;
  padding: 2px 7px; border-radius: var(--r-pill-sm);
  background: var(--overlay-scrim); border: 1px solid rgba(128,128,128,.4);
  color: var(--text); font-family: var(--font-num); font-weight: 700; font-size: var(--fs-tiny);
  white-space: nowrap; backdrop-filter: blur(2px);
}
.ov-pill-accent  { border-color: rgba(255,152,31,.35); color: var(--accent); }
.ov-pill-ok      { border-color: rgba(67,160,71,.4);   color: var(--ok); }
.ov-pill-warn    { border-color: rgba(255,179,0,.4);   color: var(--warn); }
.ov-pill-error   { border-color: rgba(229,57,53,.4);   color: var(--error); }
.ov-pill-neutral { border-color: rgba(128,128,128,.4); color: var(--text); }
/* The two canvas corner pills draw one step larger. */
.ov-pill-corner { padding: 3px 8px; font-size: var(--fs-small); }
.ov-pill-corner.ov-pill-accent { border-color: rgba(255,152,31,.4); }

.hud-stack { position: absolute; top: 42px; right: 10px; display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: flex-end; gap: 4px; animation: popIn .2s ease; }
.hud-pill { border-width: 1px; }
.hud-pill-hp     { border-color: rgba(192,57,43,.45); }
.hud-pill-prayer { border-color: rgba(41,128,185,.45); }
.hud-pill-run    { border-color: rgba(39,174,96,.45); }
.hud-label { font-family: var(--font-num); font-weight: 700; font-size: var(--fs-tiny); }
.hud-pill-hp .hud-label     { color: var(--hp-bright); }
.hud-pill-prayer .hud-label { color: var(--prayer-bright); }
.hud-pill-run .hud-label    { color: var(--run-bright); }
.hud-bar { width: 42px; height: 4px; border-radius: 2px; background: rgba(255,255,255,.14); overflow: hidden; }
.hud-fill { display: block; height: 100%; }
.hud-fill-hp { background: var(--hp); }
.hud-fill-prayer { background: var(--prayer); }
.hud-fill-run { background: var(--run); }
.hud-value { color: #fff; font-family: var(--font-num); font-weight: 700; font-size: var(--fs-tiny); }

/* The xp drop. Spawns top-centre of the canvas and remounts per tick, so the animation restarts. */
.xp-drop { position: absolute; left: 50%; top: 20%; transform: translateX(-50%); display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 5px; pointer-events: none; white-space: nowrap; animation: floatUp 2.6s ease-out forwards; }
.xp-drop-chip { width: 9px; height: 9px; border-radius: 2px; background: var(--xp-chip); border: 1px solid #000; box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px rgba(255,255,255,.25); }
.xp-drop-value {
  font-family: var(--font-num); font-weight: 700; font-size: 13px; color: #fff;
  text-shadow: -1px 0 0 #000, 1px 0 0 #000, 0 -1px 0 #000, 0 1px 0 #000, -1px -1px 0 #000, 1px 1px 0 #000, -1px 1px 0 #000, 1px -1px 0 #000;
}

/* Window chrome, shared by the trace pop-out, the bank window and (sprint entry 6) the Script
   Studio. The FAMILY DEFAULT is the neutral window; the bank's warm identity is a modifier, and
   placement is a modifier. Studio spec section 4.2 is the table these four splits come from: the
   two shipped windows disagree on ground, header, shadow and placement and on nothing else, so
   everything not listed as a modifier below is a genuine transcription both of them share. */
.window {
  position: absolute; z-index: var(--z-stage-window);
  display: flex; flex-direction: column;
  background: var(--window); border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,.08); border-radius: var(--r-window);
  box-shadow: 0 18px 50px rgba(0,0,0,.6);
  overflow: hidden; animation: popIn .18s ease;
}
/* The bank's warm identity, and the only modifier that repaints the family. */
.window-warm { background: var(--bank-ground); box-shadow: 0 20px 60px rgba(0,0,0,.65); }
.window-warm > .window-head { background: linear-gradient(180deg, var(--bank-head), var(--bank-ground)); }
/* Placement. A surface picks exactly one; `layout/<surface>.css` supplies the size. */
.window-docked  { right: 16px; bottom: 16px; }
.window-centred { left: 50%; top: 50%; transform: translate(-50%, -50%); }
.window-head { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px; padding: 8px 10px; background: var(--chrome-bar); border-bottom: 1px solid rgba(0,0,0,.5); }
.window-centred > .window-head { padding: 8px 12px; }
.window-title { color: var(--text-strong); font-weight: 650; }
.window-sub { color: var(--text-muted); }
/* Every window chrome control is 24px, which is `.btn-icon`'s geometry and not the panel
   header's 26px `.btn-icon-lg` (Task 8 Step 2). The class exists so a window's close is not a
   per-surface one-off in three layout files. */
.window-close { width: var(--ctl-h-tiny); height: var(--ctl-h-tiny); color: var(--text-muted); }
.window-close:hover { background: #2e2e2d; color: var(--text-strong); }
.window-body { min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto; }
.window-foot { padding: 6px 10px; border-top: 1px solid rgba(0,0,0,.5); color: var(--text-faint); font-size: var(--fs-micro); }
```

**On the z-index, which the plan had wrong before the reconcile pass.** The mock hard-codes `z-index:30` on the trace window and `25` on the bank window, and this task first wrote `.window` as `calc(var(--z-overlay) + 1)` with Task 16 giving the trace `+ 2`. That could not have produced the order the mock draws: `bank.css:16` puts `.bank-host` at `calc(var(--z-dialog) - 1)`, which is **49**, so a trace window at 7 would have rendered underneath it. Task 1 Step 3 replaced the guesswork with named lanes; `.window` takes `var(--z-stage-window)`, Task 17 moves `.bank-host` onto the same lane, and **two stage windows are ordered by DOM order with the most recently opened appended last** (decision D48), which is how the stage already orders siblings and is why there is no third lane for the studio or for anything after it. `layout/bank.css` and `layout/trace.css` set size and placement only; neither writes a `z-index`.

Include the boost pill (`Woodcutting +3`, accent, no bar) and the three pairing pill states (`● not paired` neutral, `● claude paired` ok, `● claude driving` accent). Every class this family defines needs a demo here or Task 21's completeness rule fails on it.

- [ ] **Step 5: Add the menu family, and stop there (decision D42)**

Sprint 3's Sound entry wants a right-click menu on a character tab with mute as its first item, and the sprint control board asks this pass to add a menu family "if it is cheap". Half of it is: the surface is the same floating language as `.ov-pill` and `.window`, so it is one prefix, one builder and one styleguide row. The other half is not, and this task ships the cheap half only.

`web/src/styles/families.ts`: add `'menu'` to `overlay.css`'s prefix list, so the family file may own it.

`overlay.css`:

```css
/* A floating menu over the stage. The family exists so sprint 3's mute item composes it rather
   than inventing a per-panel one-off; the BEHAVIOUR that opens and dismisses one is sprint 3's,
   for the reasons in the paragraph below this block. */
.menu { position: absolute; z-index: var(--z-stage-window); min-width: 132px; padding: 3px; background: var(--window); border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,.08); border-radius: var(--r-ctl); box-shadow: 0 12px 30px rgba(0,0,0,.55); animation: popIn .12s ease; }
.menu-item { display: flex; align-items: center; width: 100%; height: var(--ctl-h-quiet); padding: 0 8px; border: 0; border-radius: var(--r-ctl-sm); background: none; color: var(--text); font: inherit; text-align: left; cursor: pointer; }
.menu-item:hover:not(:disabled) { background: var(--control-hover); color: var(--text-strong); }
.menu-item:disabled { color: var(--text-disabled); cursor: not-allowed; }
```

`parts.ts`:

```ts
export interface MenuItem { label: string; onSelect(): void; disabled?: boolean }

/**
 * The markup of a floating menu, and nothing else. It is `role="menu"` with `role="menuitem"`
 * children so the consumer that opens it inherits the right semantics, and it wires each item's
 * click. It deliberately does NOT position itself, bind a document click-away, trap or restore
 * focus, or handle keys: see Task 7 Step 5's note on why that half is sprint 3's.
 */
export function menu(items: readonly MenuItem[]): HTMLElement {
  return h('div', { class: 'menu', role: 'menu' }, ...items.map(item =>
    h('button', { class: 'menu-item', type: 'button', role: 'menuitem', disabled: item.disabled === true, onclick: () => item.onSelect() }, item.label)
  ));
}
```

with a `parts.test.ts` case asserting the roles, that a disabled item is `disabled` and its `onSelect` never fires on click, and that a live item's does.

**What this task does not build, and why it is sprint 3's rather than this entry's.** The dismissal contract is the expensive half and none of it is design: a document-level click-away that does not fight the strip's click delegation in `frame/panels.ts`; an Escape close that follows ruling R30's convention (`if (event.key !== 'Escape' || event.defaultPrevented) return;`, never `preventDefault`) so one Escape still reaches the panic key; arrow-key roving and focus restore to the element that opened it; and viewport-edge positioning. On top of that, `frame/characterTabs.ts` rebuilds its whole `innerHTML` on every `render()` (`:75`, `:90`), so a menu anchored to a tab has to survive or be torn down by a rebuild that happens on every session state change. And the first consumer does not exist: there is no sound system in the shell, no `cs.` key for mute, and sprint 3's own note says mute must work **on the login screen before any character is chosen**, which is not a character tab at all. A menu shipped here would have no items in it.

So: the family ships, the interaction does not, and sprint 3 entry 10 builds `frame/contextMenu.ts` over these classes. Recorded as decision D88.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `strip.css`**

```css
/* StripButton. components/navigation/StripButton.jsx. Generated by rebuildStrip() from the
   plugin manifest; the contract is unchanged, only the button's insides and metrics move. */
.icon-strip { width: var(--strip-w); flex: none; display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 1px; padding: 6px 0; background: var(--bar); border-left: 1px solid var(--edge); }
.strip-btn { position: relative; width: 100%; height: var(--strip-btn-h); display: grid; place-items: center; border: 0; background: transparent; color: var(--glyph-rest); box-shadow: inset 2px 0 0 transparent; cursor: pointer; }
.strip-btn:hover { background: var(--chrome-hover); color: var(--text-strong); transform: scale(1.06); }
.strip-btn.active { background: var(--card); color: var(--accent); box-shadow: inset 2px 0 0 var(--accent); }
.strip-sep { flex: none; height: 1px; margin: 4px 8px; background: var(--track); }
/* Task 9 renames this to .strip-tail and moves it onto the first button of the bottom group,
   which is Plugins in the mock, not Configuration. Keep the name for now: main.ts still writes
   `strip-gear` and the two would silently disagree for one task otherwise. */
.strip-gear { margin-top: auto; }
.strip-dot { position: absolute; right: 5px; top: 5px; width: 6px; height: 6px; }
```

`.strip-dot` keeps its class and its `[data-panel="tasks"]` host, so `runBanner.test.ts:25,67,105` and `tasks.pw.test.ts:94` keep working through Task 14.

```css
/* CharTab. components/navigation/CharTab.jsx. Four slots, always. */
.char-tabs { height: var(--tabs-h); flex: none; display: flex; align-items: stretch; gap: 3px; padding: 0 8px; background: var(--chrome-bar); border-bottom: 1px solid var(--edge); }
.char-tab { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 7px; min-width: 148px; height: 100%; padding: 0 12px; border: 0; border-bottom: 2px solid transparent; border-radius: 6px 6px 0 0; background: transparent; color: var(--text-quiet); font-size: var(--fs-small); cursor: pointer; }

.char-tab:disabled { color: var(--text-disabled); cursor: not-allowed; }
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `layout/frame.css`**

Rewrite the frame regions at the mock's metrics: the title bar `height: var(--title-h)` with `linear-gradient(180deg,#232325,#1e1e20)` and `border-bottom: 1px solid rgba(0,0,0,.6)`; the wordmark `font-family: var(--font-pixel); font-size: var(--fs-brand); color: var(--accent); letter-spacing: .03em; text-shadow: 0 0 12px rgba(255,152,31,.35)`; the side panel `width: var(--panel-w); min-width: 240px; background: var(--panel); border-left: 1px solid var(--edge)` (the two width declarations arrived at Task 1 Step 6 as R2's named exceptions; this step moves them into their new home and adds the rest of the rule); the panel header `height: var(--panel-head-h); padding: 0 6px 0 12px; background: var(--bar); border-bottom: 1px solid rgba(0,0,0,.5)`; the panel body `flex:1; overflow-y:auto; padding:11px; display:flex; flex-direction:column; gap:10px`; the footer `height: var(--foot-h); padding: 0 12px; background: var(--bar); border-top: 1px solid var(--edge); font-size: var(--fs-micro); color: var(--text-muted)`; the canvas wrap and stage as the mock has them, with the canvas `border: 1px solid rgba(0,0,0,.7); border-radius: 4px; box-shadow: 0 10px 30px -12px rgba(0,0,0,.7)`.

**`.frame-body` moves with them, and it is not optional (D22, D29).** It is a shipped rule (`frame.css:20` at `30efd948`, `flex: 1; display: flex; justify-content: center; align-items: flex-start; padding: var(--sp-2)`) that this plan named nowhere before the reconcile pass, so the split could have dropped it silently. Two things about it change here rather than carrying over:

- **The outcome this step must produce is a full-height canvas column**, one whose height does not depend on the side panel's content. Today's `align-items: flex-start` makes the stage content-height, about 532px, which is why the mock's full-height side panel and full-height icon strip do not render and why a percentage height inside the canvas column resolves against the wrong box. Entry 4 (the Script Studio) asserts this outcome and fails **silently** without it: its window comes out about 508px and wobbles with the panel rather than erroring.
- **Which declaration produces it is this step's call, and Step 6 of Task 14 is why the plan does not just say `align-items: stretch`.** That step moves `#copilot-bar` out of `#canvas-wrap` to above `#stage`, so `.frame-body` ends up with two children. If it stays a centred row, `align-items: stretch` is the change; if it becomes a column, the height comes from `.stage { flex: 1 }` and the cross-axis property is the panel width instead. Pick one, and keep `.canvas-wrap { align-items: flex-start }` either way so the game canvas stays pinned to the top rather than stretching.

Every panel's root child animates in: add `.panel-body > * { animation: fadeUp .18s ease; }` here rather than in each panel, which is exactly what a layout file is for.

Keep both responsive breakpoints (1100px and 700px) with the new panel width substituted.

- [ ] **Step 6: Fix the character tab status class**

`web/src/frame/characterTabs.ts:75` currently writes `<span class="char-tab-status" data-tab-status>${slot.status}</span>`. The online case becomes mono in Task 18; for now, add the class only when the status is a clock, which it never is yet:

```ts
      // `clock` is a BOOLEAN marker, not a string: "this tab renders a clock". An empty-string
      // marker would be falsy here and the `num` class would never be added, which is the exact
      // bug Task 18's own test would then fail on.
      const clockAttr = typeof slot.onlineSince === 'number' ? ` data-online-since="${slot.onlineSince}"` : '';
      ...
      <span class="char-tab-status${slot.clock ? ' num' : ''}"${clockAttr} data-tab-status>${escapeHtml(slot.status)}</span>
```

with `clock?: boolean` and `onlineSince?: number | null` added to the `character` arm of `TabSlot`, both left `undefined` by `computeSlots` until Task 18. The status text stays `slot.status` at render time and `tick()` overwrites it for the tabs that carry `data-online-since`, so `render()` stays deterministic and `computeSlots` stays pure and free of a clock. Task 18 ends `render()` with a `tick(deps.now())` call so an online tab never paints a blank status for one frame.

`characterTabs.test.ts`'s existing assertions on the literal `'online'` keep passing, which is the point: this task changes chrome, not behaviour.

### Task 9: The icon set and the manifest narrowing (G7)

Eleven drawn 16x16 SVGs replace the emoji strip. Narrowing `PluginManifest['icon']` to the glyph union is what turns "we changed the icons" into a compile error listing every site that still needs one, which is the cheapest possible migration: `map-tests.md` section 5 counts **24 typed sites across 6 files** that fail to compile and **6 untyped fixture sites across 2 files** that do not, and the untyped ones would keep rendering emoji into a strip that should be drawing SVG.

The glyph names are **display** names, not plugin ids: `automation` is plugin id `tasks` and `claude` is plugin id `connect`. They must not be conflated with `PanelId`.

Design source: `map-design.md` section 2.7 (the eleven paths, verbatim) and section 3.20 (the strip, including its two separators and the spacer).

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/ui/icon.ts`, `web/src/ui/icon.test.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/styles/icon.css`, `web/src/styles/strip.css` (the `.strip-tail` spacer), `web/src/plugins/types.ts:15-29`, `web/src/frame/panels.ts:4`, `web/src/main.ts` (every `shell.register` icon, `rebuildStrip`, `onPanelChange`)
- Modify: the manifests at `builtin/xpTracker.ts:42-47`, `lootTracker.ts:39-43`, `notes.ts:21-24`, `screenshot.ts:57-60`, `statusHud.ts:40-44`, `tasks.ts:363-393` (**moved by SP4b Task 9's two new settings; the `icon: '▶'` is at `:364`**), `marketplace.ts:167-170`, `bank.ts:24-28`, `characters.ts:28`, `panels/account.ts:6`, `panels/connect.ts:12`
- Test: `web/src/ui/icon.test.ts` (create), `web/src/panels/account.test.ts:17`, `panels/connect.test.ts:19`, `builtin/marketplace.test.ts:101`, `frame/panels.test.ts:4`, `plugins/pluginsPanel.test.ts:16,17,18,80`, `plugins/registry.test.ts` (twelve sites), `plugins/types.test.ts:8,15`, `ui/strip.test.ts:6,7,8`, `frame/runBanner.test.ts:9,57,77` (three `▶` fixture buttons; SP4b's new cases pushed the last two down by one)

- Produces:
  - `export type IconName = 'automation' | 'claude' | 'xp' | 'loot' | 'events' | 'notes' | 'screenshot' | 'bank' | 'account' | 'plugins' | 'config';`

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { ICON_NAMES, icon, type IconName } from './icon';

describe('icon', () => {
  it('ships exactly the eleven glyphs the design bundle declares, in its order', () => {
    expect(ICON_NAMES).toEqual([
      'automation', 'claude', 'xp', 'loot', 'events',
      'notes', 'screenshot', 'bank', 'account', 'plugins', 'config'

  it('draws a 16x16 currentColor svg with a 1.5 stroke', () => {

    expect(el.getAttribute('height')).toBe('16');
    expect(el.getAttribute('viewBox')).toBe('0 0 16 16');

    expect(el.getAttribute('stroke')).toBe('currentColor');
    expect(el.getAttribute('stroke-width')).toBe('1.5');
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write `icon.ts`**

Copy the eleven glyph bodies **verbatim** from `docs/design/idlescape-shell-v2/components/core/Icon.jsx`; the last test case is what proves you did. The bodies below are that file's, with JSX attribute names converted to their SVG spelling.

```ts
// web/src/ui/icon.ts -- the one drawn icon set. 16x16, currentColor, 1.5px stroke, geometric.
//
// The glyph names are DISPLAY names, not plugin ids: `automation` is plugin id `tasks` and
// `claude` is plugin id `connect`. Do not conflate this union with PanelId.
// Paths copied verbatim from docs/design/idlescape-shell-v2/components/core/Icon.jsx; the test
// diffs them against that file, so edit the bundle, never this list.

export type IconName =
  | 'automation' | 'claude' | 'xp' | 'loot' | 'events'
  | 'notes' | 'screenshot' | 'bank' | 'account' | 'plugins' | 'config';

export const ICON_NAMES: readonly IconName[] = [
  'automation', 'claude', 'xp', 'loot', 'events',
  'notes', 'screenshot', 'bank', 'account', 'plugins', 'config'
];

const GLYPHS: Record<IconName, string> = {
  automation: '<path d="M5 3.5 12 8 5 12.5 Z" fill="currentColor" stroke="none"/>',
  claude: '<path d="M8 1.5 9.7 6.3 14.5 8 9.7 9.7 8 14.5 6.3 9.7 1.5 8 6.3 6.3Z" fill="currentColor" stroke="none"/>',
  xp: '<polyline points="2,12.5 6,7.5 9,10 14,3.5"/>',
  loot: '<g><circle cx="8" cy="8" r="5.5"/><circle cx="8" cy="8" r="1.8"/></g>',
  events: '<path d="M1.5 8.5h3l2-4.5 3 8 2-3.5h3"/>',
  notes: '<path d="M3.5 4.5h9M3.5 8h9M3.5 11.5h6"/>',
  screenshot: '<g><rect x="2" y="4.5" width="12" height="8.5" rx="1.5"/><circle cx="8" cy="8.7" r="2.2"/></g>',
  bank: '<g><path d="M2.5 6.5 8 3l5.5 3.5"/><path d="M4.5 8v3.5M8 8v3.5M11.5 8v3.5M2.5 13.5h11"/></g>',
  account: '<g><circle cx="5.5" cy="5.5" r="2.5"/><path d="M2 13.5c.7-2.8 2-4 3.5-4s2.8 1.2 3.5 4"/><circle cx="11" cy="6.5" r="2"/><path d="M11.5 9.6c1.3.3 2 1.5 2.4 3.9"/></g>',
  plugins: '<g><rect x="2.5" y="2.5" width="4.5" height="4.5"/><rect x="9" y="2.5" width="4.5" height="4.5"/><rect x="2.5" y="9" width="4.5" height="4.5"/><rect x="9" y="9" width="4.5" height="4.5" fill="currentColor" stroke="none"/></g>',
  config: '<g><path d="M2.5 5h1.6M7.8 5h5.7M2.5 11h5.7M12.1 11h1.4"/><circle cx="6" cy="5" r="1.7"/><circle cx="10.2" cy="11" r="1.7"/></g>'
};

const isIconName = (name: string): name is IconName => name in GLYPHS;

/**
 * One glyph as an `<svg>`. An unknown name falls back to `plugins` rather than rendering nothing,
 * so a third-party manifest can never produce a blank strip button (companion spec G7).
 */
export function icon(name: string, size = 16): SVGSVGElement {
  const el = document.createElementNS('http://www.w3.org/2000/svg', 'svg');
  el.setAttribute('class', 'icon');
  el.setAttribute('width', String(size));
  el.setAttribute('height', String(size));
  el.setAttribute('viewBox', '0 0 16 16');
  el.setAttribute('fill', 'none');
  el.setAttribute('stroke', 'currentColor');
  el.setAttribute('stroke-width', '1.5');
  el.setAttribute('aria-hidden', 'true');
  el.innerHTML = GLYPHS[isIconName(name) ? name : 'plugins'];
  return el;
}
```

`innerHTML` on an SVG element is a static string from this module, never player input; the same pattern is already used at `frame/overlays.ts:2`.

- [ ] **Step 6: Render the glyph in the strip and the panel header**

`web/src/main.ts`, in `rebuildStrip`, replace `btn.textContent = m.icon;` with:

```ts
    btn.replaceChildren(icon(m.icon));
```

and insert the two separators and the spacer the mock's strip has (`map-design.md` section 3.20): after `claude`, after `screenshot`, and a `<span class="strip-sep">` for each.

**The spacer is not `.strip-gear`.** `main.ts:252` puts that class on `config` alone and `frame.css:45` is `.strip-gear { margin-top: auto }`, so today only the gear drops to the bottom. The mock puts the `flex:1` spacer **before Plugins**, with both Plugins and Configuration sitting at the bottom. `.strip-gear` carries nothing else: its only two rules are that `margin-top` and the horizontal-breakpoint `margin: 0 0 0 auto` at `frame.css:70`. So rename the class rather than adding a second one, and put it on the head of the tail group:

```css
/* strip.css, replacing .strip-gear: the spacer belongs to the FIRST button of the bottom group,
   not to the gear, or Plugins stays up with Bank and Account. */
.strip-tail { margin-top: auto; }
/* and inside the horizontal breakpoint, where the strip is a row: */
  .strip-tail { margin: 0 0 0 auto; }
```

Retire `strip-gear` in the same pass: `main.ts:252`, `web/styleguide.html:398` and its `.sg-code` strip at `:404`. Grep for it afterwards and expect nothing.

Then build both the spacer and the separators from position, never from `some()`:

```ts
  // The mock groups the strip: Automation and Claude, then the trackers and tools, then the
  // account surfaces, then the two system panels pinned to the bottom.
  const GROUP_AFTER = new Set(['connect', 'screenshot']);
  const TAIL = new Set(['plugins', 'config']);
  ...
  let tailSeen = false;
  enabledPanels.forEach((m, i) => {
    ...
    const classes = ['strip-btn'];
    if (TAIL.has(m.id) && !tailSeen) { classes.push('strip-tail'); tailSeen = true; }
    btn.className = classes.join(' ');
    strip.appendChild(btn);
    // "Is there a later button", not "does another button exist anywhere": `some(n => n.id !==
    // m.id)` is true whenever the list has more than one entry, so it never prevents the
    // trailing rule it was written for.
    if (GROUP_AFTER.has(m.id) && i < enabledPanels.length - 1) {
      strip.appendChild(h('span', { class: 'strip-sep', 'aria-hidden': 'true' }));
    }
  });
```

A separator carries no `[data-panel]`, so `applyTabListA11y`'s `tabs()` query skips it and the roving tabindex is unaffected. Add a case to `web/src/ui/strip.test.ts` proving exactly that, with a separator in the fixture.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing `cs.panel` fallback test (ruling C3, R20)**

`web/src/frame/panels.test.ts`. There is **no test anywhere** for this fallback today, and it is permanent behaviour, not a migration step.

The file has exactly one helper, `function fixture()` at `panels.test.ts:7`, returning `{ strip, panel, title, body }`; there is no `setupRegistry` and no `registry` field, and each existing case builds its own registry with `createPluginRegistry(els)` (`:21`). Its `beforeEach` clears `document.body` and **not** `localStorage`, so these two cases have to clear it themselves or they leak into every case after them.

```ts
const ACCOUNT_MANIFEST: PluginManifest = { id: 'account', name: 'Account', icon: 'account', tier: 'shell' };

test('a stored characters panel id opens Account, permanently', () => {
  localStorage.clear();
  const els = fixture();
  const pc = createPluginRegistry(els);
  pc.register(ACCOUNT_MANIFEST, { title: 'Account', mount: body => { body.textContent = 'account'; } });
  localStorage.setItem('cs.panel', 'characters');
  pc.restore();
  expect(els.title.textContent).toBe('Account');
  expect(els.panel.classList.contains('hidden')).toBe(false);
  // And the stored value self-heals to the id that now exists, because open() rewrites it.
  expect(localStorage.getItem('cs.panel')).toBe('account');
});

test('an unknown stored panel id opens nothing and leaves the key alone', () => {
  localStorage.clear();
  const els = fixture();
  const pc = createPluginRegistry(els);
  pc.register(XP_MANIFEST, { title: 'XP Tracker', mount: () => {} });
  localStorage.setItem('cs.panel', 'not-a-panel');
  pc.restore();
  expect(els.panel.classList.contains('hidden')).toBe(true);
  expect(localStorage.getItem('cs.panel')).toBe('not-a-panel');
});
```

The fixture's `<nav id="strip">` carries `data-panel="connect"` and `data-panel="xp"` buttons only; neither case needs an `account` button, because `restore()` opens through `views`, not through the strip.

Add `localStorage.clear()` to the file's `beforeEach` at the same time, so the two new cases cannot leak `cs.panel` into the six that already exist.

Run: `cd web && npx vitest run src/frame/panels.test.ts` - expect FAIL on the first case.

- [ ] **Step 2: Implement the fallback**

`web/src/frame/panels.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * Panel ids that no longer exist, and where their players should land instead.
 *
 * This is permanent, not a migration step: `cs.panel` is only ever rewritten by a successful
 * `open()`, so a browser that has not been used since before the Characters merge can arrive at
 * any time, for as long as the key exists. Adding an entry here is cheaper than the alternative,
 * which is a player whose shell silently opens no panel at all.
 */
export const PANEL_ALIASES: Record<string, PanelId> = { characters: 'account' };

/** Every id `views` can hold, as data, so an arbitrary stored string can be narrowed and not asserted. */
export const PANEL_IDS: readonly PanelId[] = ['tasks', 'connect', 'xp', 'loot', 'events', 'notes', 'screenshot', 'bank', 'marketplace', 'account', 'plugins', 'config'];

  restore() {
    let saved: string | null = null;
    try { saved = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY); } catch { /* storage blocked */ }
    if (!saved) return;
    const aliased = PANEL_ALIASES[saved] ?? saved;
    const id = PANEL_IDS.find(p => p === aliased) ?? null;
    if (id !== null && views.has(id)) open(id);
  }
```

**No `as PanelId`.** Ruling R20 exists precisely because the stored value is an arbitrary string out of an old browser: `const id = (PANEL_ALIASES[saved] ?? saved) as PanelId` is the assertion the ruling rejects, and it is what today's `views.has(saved as PanelId)` at `panels.ts:60` already does. `PANEL_IDS` is a `readonly PanelId[]`, so deleting a member of the union is a compile error here too, which is the second half of what makes C3's deletion safe.

`open()` writes `cs.panel` itself, so the stored value self-heals after one open, which is what the first test's last assertion pins.

- [ ] **Step 5: Rename Tasks to Automation (G6) and fold Marketplace into a tab (G4)**

`builtin/tasks.ts:364`: `name: 'Tasks'` becomes `name: 'Automation'`, `icon: '▶'` becomes `'automation'`, `description` becomes `'Run scripts, browse the marketplace, and watch a live run.'`. **The id stays `tasks`.** The `PanelView`'s own `title: 'Tasks'` at `:338` becomes `'Automation'` in the same edit; it is what `#panel-title` renders and what `tasks.pw.test.ts:44` asserts after Step 8.

Two strings inside the marketplace still say "the Tasks tab" and become wrong the moment it is a tab of this panel rather than a panel beside it. Both are SP4b Task 9's disabled-script copy and both are in `marketplace.ts`:

- `:65`, `alert('This script is turned off. Turn it back on in the Tasks tab.', ...)` becomes `'This script is turned off. Turn it back on under My tasks.'`
- `:72`, the Run button's `off` label `'Open Tasks'` becomes `'Open My tasks'`, and `handlers.openTasks()` at `:128` stops calling `deps.openPanel('tasks')` (which is now a no-op, because the player is already in that panel) and calls a new `MarketplaceDeps.showMyTasks?: () => void` instead. The Automation panel passes `() => { tab = 'my'; paintTabs(); paintContent(); }`; `createMarketplacePlugin` leaves it undefined and `openTasks` falls back to `deps.openPanel('tasks')`, so the standalone view keeps working for as long as it exists. Update `marketplace.test.ts`'s disabled-card cases to the new copy in the same commit.

Above the existing content, mount the segmented control and switch on it. The tab lives in the plugin's `panel()` closure, not in `localStorage`: the `PanelView` object is built once per plugin enable (`registry.ts:47`) and only its `mount`/`unmount` pair runs per open, so a closure variable survives a re-open without adding a `cs.` key (ruling R27).

```ts
    let tab: 'my' | 'market' = 'my';
    const tabHost = h('div');
    const contentHost = h('div', { class: 'automation-content' });
    function paintTabs(): void {
      tabHost.replaceChildren(segmented(
        [{ value: 'my', label: 'My tasks' }, { value: 'market', label: 'Marketplace' }],
        tab,
        next => { tab = next as 'my' | 'market'; paintTabs(); paintContent(); }
      ));
    }
    let marketMounted = false;
    function paintContent(): void {
      if (tab === 'market') { market.mount(contentHost); marketMounted = true; return; }
      // Guarded: the default tab is 'my', so the FIRST paint would otherwise unmount a view that
      // has never mounted, and this shell's unmount() contract assumes a prior mount().
      if (marketMounted) { market.unmount?.(); marketMounted = false; }
      contentHost.replaceChildren(myTasksEl);
    }
```

`myTasksEl` is what today's `mountPanel` passes to `body.replaceChildren(...)` at `tasks.ts:305-312`: `runHost`, `section('My scripts', scriptsHost)`, `section('Run snippet', ...)`, `section('History', historyHost, reportHost)` and the `.card-foot` "Browse the Marketplace" link. Wrap the first four in one `h('div', { class: 'stack' })`, give it the name `myTasksEl`, and keep it in the closure rather than mounting it into `body` directly, so `paintContent` can swap it for the marketplace view and back. **The `.card-foot` link is deleted here, not moved**: it is the last consumer of `.card-foot` outside the screen family Task 2 Step 0 renamed, its `data-open-marketplace` hook is the subject of the test Step 7 rewrites, and a link that opens the tab the player is looking at is worse than no link.

`market` is `createMarketplaceView(ctx, deps.marketplace)`, built from the Automation panel's own `ctx` and the deps the Interfaces block above says have to be extracted first; `TasksPluginDeps` gains the `marketplace: MarketplaceDeps` field and `main.ts:202` passes it, adding `showMyTasks` to that object literal. `unmount()` on the Automation panel must call `market.unmount?.()` (through the same `marketMounted` guard) as well as its own teardown, or the marketplace's 150 ms search debounce (`marketplace.ts:152`) outlives the panel.

`builtin/marketplace.ts`: drop `panel` from the returned `ShellPlugin` so `emitStrip` (`registry.ts:36-38`) stops giving it a button. Keep the manifest, the id, `defaultEnabled: true` and the exported view factory. That is the whole of G4's strip half.

`main.ts`: the Automation plugin registration gains the marketplace deps; the `marketplace` registration stays exactly where it is in the order, now contributing no button.

### Task 11: The frame event bus, the pairing singleton, and the six producers (G1, G2)

The Events panel is not a panel that needs building; it is a subsystem that needs designing, with a panel on top. This task builds the subsystem: a frame-level, per-account, append-only ring buffer with one `emit` entry point, plus the six producers that call it. Session-scoped and not persisted (ruling C5), which removes every migration and quota question.

Two of the six producers only run while their surface is open today, and both must become frame-lifetime singletons (ruling R10). The run producer must attach **per session**, at the point `frame/stage.ts` already holds the raw `TasksApi`, because `tasks/router.ts:174-175` drops both status and trace for background characters and a cross-character feed cannot use it (ruling R9).

Contract source: `map-contracts.md` sections 4, 4.1, 4.2 and 8.5 to 8.7.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/frame/events.ts`, `web/src/frame/events.test.ts`, `web/src/frame/eventProducers.ts`, `web/src/frame/eventProducers.test.ts`, `web/src/frame/pairing.ts`, `web/src/frame/pairing.test.ts`, `web/src/frame/singletons.ts`, `web/src/frame/singletons.test.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/tasks/api.harness.ts` (the strict `fakeTasksApi` the producer tests need), `web/src/ui/copy.ts` (nothing: `bankUpdated` already shipped in Task 4; check it is there before writing `bankProducer`)
- Modify: `web/src/main.ts` (build the three singletons, hand them down), `web/src/frame/stage.ts:143-182` (the `makeSessions` block; the per-session run producer attaches inside its `onReady` at `:146-176`), `web/src/sessions/wire.ts:30-51` (xp, loot, level producers), `web/src/panels/connect.ts:212-217` and `:244-245` (consume the pairing singleton instead of owning the subscription), `web/src/bank/view.ts:245,266,304` (three `store.start()`/`.stop()` calls, not two: `:304` is the window's `destroy`) and `web/src/plugins/builtin/bank.ts:52,59` (stop starting and stopping the store)
- Test: the three new test files, plus `web/src/panels/connect.test.ts` (the panel now takes a store)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `SKILL_NAMES` from `web/src/stats/skills.ts`; `XpEvent`, `InventoryEvent` from `web/src/clientTypes.ts`; `TasksApi` from `web/src/tasks/api.ts`.
- Produces:

```ts
// web/src/frame/events.ts
export type ShellEventType = 'xp' | 'loot' | 'level' | 'run' | 'claude' | 'bank';
export type ShellEventTone = 'default' | 'fail';
export interface ShellEvent {
  readonly seq: number;
  readonly at: number;
  readonly characterId: string | null;   // null for account-scoped events (bank, gateway)
  readonly characterName: string | null;
  readonly type: ShellEventType;
  readonly skill: string | null;         // a SKILL_NAMES display name, or null
  readonly text: string;
  readonly tone: ShellEventTone;
  /** The numeric payload the canvas xp drop reads: xp gained, items added, or null. */
  readonly amount: number | null;
}
export interface EventBus {
  emit(e: Omit<ShellEvent, 'seq' | 'at'> & { at?: number }): void;
  all(): readonly ShellEvent[];
  subscribe(fn: (e: ShellEvent) => void): () => void;
  dispose(): void;
}
export const EVENT_CAP = 500;
export function createEventBus(opts?: { now?(): number; cap?: number }): EventBus;
```

**Four of `ShellEvent`'s fields are a cross-sprint contract and none of them may be narrowed away.** Sprint 3's Sound entry reads this feed to decide what to play, and the sprint control board records its requirement on entry 4 as "the event feed's event shape carries kind, character, time and a one-line label". Those are `type` (the kind), `characterId` with `characterName` (the character, both nullable for an account-scoped event), `at` (the time, a unix millisecond stamp the producer may override), and `text` (the one-line label, already rendered and already the thing the Events row prints and `Copy for Claude` copies). They are all present as written above, so this is a constraint on later edits rather than new work: an implementer who folds `text` into a render-time formatter, or drops `characterName` because `characterId` is enough for the panel, breaks a consumer that does not exist yet. `tone`, `skill`, `amount` and `seq` carry no such promise and may change.

```ts
// web/src/frame/pairing.ts
export type PairingState = 'unpaired' | 'paired-stale' | 'paired-live';
export interface PairingStore {
  state(): PairingState;
  sessions(): readonly AgentTokenRow[];
  subscribe(fn: (state: PairingState) => void): () => void;
  start(uid: string): void;
  stop(): void;
}
export function createPairingStore(deps: PairingDeps): PairingStore;
```

```ts
// web/src/frame/eventProducers.ts
export function xpProducer(bus, deps): (ev: XpEvent) => void;
export function lootProducer(bus, deps): (ev: InventoryEvent) => void;
export function runProducer(bus, api: TasksApi, deps): () => void;   // returns its disposer
export function pairingProducer(bus, store: PairingStore): () => void;
export function bankProducer(bus, store: BankStore): () => void;
```

- [ ] **Step 0: Give the frame-lifetime singletons a home before adding three more**

`main.ts` is 358 lines at `1bfdc7d`, the Global Constraints put every file under 400, and this task alone adds the event bus, the pairing store, the `/health` poll lifted out of `connect.ts`, the bank store's start/stop and five producer wirings to it. Tasks 13, 14, 16 and 19 add more. Do the extraction first, as its own commit, so no later task has to stop and do a file split it has no reason to be doing:

```ts
// web/src/frame/singletons.ts -- everything in the shell that outlives a panel.
//
// Three of these were owned by a surface that opens and closes (ruling R10): the agentTokens
// subscription lived in the Claude panel's mount(), the bank store was started by the bank
// WINDOW, and the /health poll ran only while the Claude panel was open. An append-only feed fed
// by producers that run only while the player is looking at them has a hole in the middle of
// every session, and the co-pilot bar needs the pairing truth with the panel shut.
export interface FrameSingletons {
  bus: EventBus;
  pairing: PairingStore;
  bank: BankStore;
  health(): HealthState;
  start(uid: string): void;
  stop(): void;
  dispose(): void;
}
export function createFrameSingletons(deps: FrameSingletonDeps): FrameSingletons;
```

`start(uid)` starts the pairing store, the bank store and the health poll and attaches the pairing and bank producers; `stop()` reverses all five, exactly once each, and is what sign-out calls. `main.ts` builds it once and hands the pieces down. Task 10 has already moved the register block to `frame/registerPanels.ts`; between the two, `main.ts` ends this plan smaller than it started.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing bus test**

`web/src/frame/events.test.ts`. The cap and the teardown are the two things a ring buffer gets wrong.

```ts
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import { EVENT_CAP, createEventBus } from './events';

// `amount` is `number | null` on ShellEvent with no `?`, so a fixture that omits it does not
// typecheck and `npm run typecheck` fails before a single case runs.
const base = { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'shoth4019zr', type: 'xp' as const, skill: 'Woodcutting', tone: 'default' as const, amount: null };
let clock = 1000;
beforeEach(() => { clock = 1000; });
const bus = () => createEventBus({ now: () => clock++ });

describe('createEventBus', () => {
  it('stamps a monotonic seq and the clock, newest last', () => {
    const b = bus();
    b.emit({ ...base, text: 'first' });
    b.emit({ ...base, text: 'second' });
    expect(b.all().map(e => [e.seq, e.at, e.text])).toEqual([[1, 1000, 'first'], [2, 1001, 'second']]);
  });

  it('honours an explicit at, for a producer that knows better than the clock', () => {
    const b = bus();
    b.emit({ ...base, text: 'x', at: 42 });
    expect(b.all()[0].at).toBe(42);
  });

  it('drops the oldest past the cap and keeps seq climbing', () => {
    const b = createEventBus({ now: () => 0, cap: 3 });
    for (const text of ['a', 'b', 'c', 'd']) b.emit({ ...base, text });
    expect(b.all().map(e => e.text)).toEqual(['b', 'c', 'd']);
    expect(b.all().map(e => e.seq)).toEqual([2, 3, 4]);
  });

  it('defaults to a 500 event cap', () => {
    expect(EVENT_CAP).toBe(500);
    const b = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
    for (let i = 0; i < EVENT_CAP + 10; i++) b.emit({ ...base, text: String(i) });
    expect(b.all().length).toBe(EVENT_CAP);
    expect(b.all()[0].text).toBe('10');
  });

  it('notifies every subscriber with the stamped record, and stops on unsubscribe', () => {
    const b = bus();
    const seen: string[] = [];
    const off = b.subscribe(e => seen.push(`${e.seq}:${e.text}`));
    b.emit({ ...base, text: 'a' });
    off();
    b.emit({ ...base, text: 'b' });
    expect(seen).toEqual(['1:a']);
    expect(b.all().length).toBe(2);          // unsubscribing does not stop recording
  });

  it('fires nothing after dispose, and answers an empty log', () => {
    const b = bus();
    const fn = vi.fn();
    b.subscribe(fn);
    b.emit({ ...base, text: 'a' });
    b.dispose();
    b.emit({ ...base, text: 'b' });
    expect(fn).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);      // not twice: dispose fences what comes next
    expect(b.all()).toEqual([]);
  });

  it('survives a subscriber that throws, so one bad consumer cannot stop the feed', () => {
    const b = bus();
    const good: string[] = [];
    b.subscribe(() => { throw new Error('boom'); });
    b.subscribe(e => good.push(e.text));
    expect(() => b.emit({ ...base, text: 'a' })).not.toThrow();
    expect(good).toEqual(['a']);
  });
});
```

Run: `cd web && npx vitest run src/frame/events.test.ts` - expect FAIL.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the bus**

```ts
// web/src/frame/events.ts -- the cross-character event feed the Events panel reads.
//
// It sits in the frame, above SP7's session manager, because it is cross-character and no
// per-character iframe session can see the others. It is session-scoped and NOT persisted: the
// panel's own footer says "this session", and not persisting removes every migration and quota
// question (companion spec G1). Capped, append-only, one emit() entry point.
export type ShellEventType = 'xp' | 'loot' | 'level' | 'run' | 'claude' | 'bank';
export type ShellEventTone = 'default' | 'fail';

export interface ShellEvent {
  readonly seq: number;
  readonly at: number;
  /** null for an account-scoped event: the bank, and the gateway's own health. */
  readonly characterId: string | null;
  readonly characterName: string | null;
  readonly type: ShellEventType;
  /** A SKILL_NAMES display name. The skill filter is a separate axis from the type. */
  readonly skill: string | null;
  readonly text: string;
  /** `fail` is a run that ended badly: a red rail and amber text, not a red type. */
  readonly tone: ShellEventTone;
  /** The numeric payload the canvas xp drop reads (Task 13): xp gained, items added, or null. */
  readonly amount: number | null;
}

export interface EventBus {
  emit(e: Omit<ShellEvent, 'seq' | 'at'> & { at?: number }): void;
  all(): readonly ShellEvent[];
  subscribe(fn: (e: ShellEvent) => void): () => void;
  dispose(): void;
}

export const EVENT_CAP = 500;

export function createEventBus(opts: { now?(): number; cap?: number } = {}): EventBus {
  const now = opts.now ?? (() => Date.now());
  const cap = opts.cap ?? EVENT_CAP;
  const log: ShellEvent[] = [];
  const subs = new Set<(e: ShellEvent) => void>();
  let seq = 0;
  let disposed = false;

  return {
    emit(e) {
      if (disposed) return;
      const record: ShellEvent = { ...e, seq: ++seq, at: e.at ?? now() };
      log.push(record);
      if (log.length > cap) log.splice(0, log.length - cap);
      for (const fn of [...subs]) {
        // One consumer throwing must not stop the feed: the panel is a consumer and so is the
        // canvas drop, and neither owns the other.
        try { fn(record); } catch { /* a consumer's problem, not the bus's */ }
      }
    },
    all: () => log,
    subscribe(fn) { subs.add(fn); return () => { subs.delete(fn); }; },
    dispose() { disposed = true; subs.clear(); log.length = 0; }
  };
}
```

Run to green.

- [ ] **Step 3: Lift the pairing subscription out of the Claude panel (ruling R10)**

`web/src/frame/pairing.ts` takes `defaultSubscribeSessions` out of `panels/connect.ts:44-53` unchanged: the same `agentTokens where uid == uid` query, the same `AgentTokenRow` shape, the same `SEEN_RECENTLY_MS = 60_000`. It derives the three-value state that `map-contracts.md` section 1.2 identifies:

```ts
export type PairingState = 'unpaired' | 'paired-stale' | 'paired-live';

/** `activeRows()` at connect.ts:103-105, and the liveness test at :153, lifted verbatim. */
function deriveState(rows: readonly AgentTokenRow[], now: number): PairingState {
  const active = rows.filter(r => r.revokedAt === null);
  if (active.length === 0) return 'unpaired';
  return active.some(r => r.lastSeenAt !== null && now - r.lastSeenAt <= SEEN_RECENTLY_MS)
    ? 'paired-live' : 'paired-stale';
}
```

`panels/connect.ts` takes the store as a dependency and renders from `store.sessions()`, subscribing in `mount` and unsubscribing in `unmount` as it does today; only the *ownership* of the Firestore listener moves. `main.ts` builds it beside `bankStore` and calls `start(uid)` from the same place that already knows the uid.

Its test must include the case that motivates the whole move:

```ts
it('keeps reporting after every consumer unsubscribes, because the bar needs it with the panel shut', () => {
  const store = createPairingStore(deps);
  store.start('uid1');
  const off = store.subscribe(() => {});
  off();
  deps.pushSnapshot([{ id: 't1', label: 'MacBook', createdAt: 1, lastSeenAt: 1, revokedAt: null }]);
  expect(store.state()).toBe('paired-live');
});
it('stops the underlying listener exactly once, on stop()', () => {
  const store = createPairingStore(deps);
  store.start('uid1');
  store.stop(); store.stop();
  expect(deps.unsubscribeCalls).toBe(1);
});
```

Move both calls to `main.ts`: start when the identity resolves, stop on sign-out and on `dispose`. The window and the panel become pure consumers of `store.subscribe`.
