- [ ] **Step 5: Write the failing producer tests**

`web/src/frame/eventProducers.test.ts`. Every fake here is as strict as the real thing: `map-contracts.md` gives the real payload shapes and this file uses them, not loose objects.

**`fakeTasksApi` does not exist yet; build it in `web/src/tasks/api.harness.ts`**, beside the fakes that are already there, and not as a fresh object literal in the test. It implements the **whole** `TasksApi` surface, so a method added upstream is a compile error here rather than a silent `undefined` at a call site, which is the honesty this task's lede claims. It adds exactly two methods of its own:

```ts
/** Push one trace event to every current `onEvent` subscriber, as the recorder would. */
pushEvent(e: TraceEvent): void;
/** How many `onEvent` subscribers are live. A disposer that only gates would leave this at 1. */
eventSubscribers(): number;
```

`api.harness.ts` imports nothing from vitest (see its own header comment on why), so `pushEvent` is a plain function over a `Set`, not a spy.

Two payload shapes in the cases below have to match `web/src/tasks/types.ts` exactly or the file does not compile: `run_done` is `{ ... xpGained: Record<string, number>; itemsDelta: Record<number, number>; tasksEntered: string[] }` (`types.ts:269`), so `xpGained` is `{}` and `tasksEntered` is `[]`, never `0` and `1`. `TraceEvent` itself is `types.ts:255-273`, an eighteen-arm union; the producer switches on `kind` and must not assume any other arm's fields.

```ts
describe('xpProducer', () => {
  it('emits one xp event per hook event, with the skill DISPLAY name', () => {
    const bus = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
    const on = xpProducer(bus, { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'shoth4019zr', levelOf: () => 34 });
    on({ skill: 0, xp: 4180, level: 34, delta: 25 });   // skill 0 is Attack in SKILL_NAMES
    const [e] = bus.all();
    // `amount` is the field the canvas xp drop reads (Task 13 does `e.amount ?? 0`), so it is
    // pinned here rather than left to a regex over `text` later.
    expect(e).toMatchObject({ type: 'xp', characterId: 'c1', skill: 'Attack', text: '+25 Attack xp', amount: 25 });
  });

  it('emits a SECOND event, of type level, when the level moved', () => {
    const bus = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
    let known = 34;
    const on = xpProducer(bus, { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'a', levelOf: () => known });
    on({ skill: 8, xp: 100, level: 35, delta: 25 });
    expect(bus.all().map(e => e.type)).toEqual(['xp', 'level']);
    expect(bus.all()[1].text).toBe('Level up! Woodcutting 34 → 35');
    // Mutation target: emitting the level event again for the same level must fail this.
    known = 35;
    on({ skill: 8, xp: 125, level: 35, delta: 25 });
    expect(bus.all().map(e => e.type)).toEqual(['xp', 'level', 'xp']);
  });

  it('emits nothing for a zero or negative delta', () => {
    const bus = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
    xpProducer(bus, { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'a', levelOf: () => 1 })({ skill: 0, xp: 0, level: 1, delta: 0 });
    expect(bus.all()).toEqual([]);
  });
});

describe('runProducer', () => {
  it('emits on run_started and run_done, and marks a failure with the fail tone', () => {
    const bus = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
    const api = fakeTasksApi();                 // strict: onEvent and onStatus both return disposers
    runProducer(bus, api, { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'alt_miner' });
    api.pushEvent({ seq: 1, at: 0, kind: 'run_started', runId: 'r1', scriptId: 'mine-and-drop', version: 1, params: {}, startedBy: 'claude' });
    api.pushEvent({ seq: 2, at: 0, kind: 'run_done', status: 'failed', summary: 'needs a bronze pickaxe', durationMs: 1, xpGained: {}, itemsDelta: {}, tasksEntered: [] });
    expect(bus.all().map(e => [e.type, e.tone, e.text])).toEqual([
      ['claude', 'default', 'Claude started mine-and-drop'],
      ['run', 'fail', 'mine-and-drop failed — needs a bronze pickaxe']
    ]);
  });

  it('emits a run event, not a claude event, when the player started it', () => {
    const bus = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
    const api = fakeTasksApi();
    runProducer(bus, api, { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'a' });
    api.pushEvent({ seq: 1, at: 0, kind: 'run_started', runId: 'r1', scriptId: 's', version: 1, params: {}, startedBy: 'player' });
    expect(bus.all()[0].type).toBe('run');
  });

  it('fires nothing after its disposer runs', () => {
    const bus = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
    const api = fakeTasksApi();
    runProducer(bus, api, { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'a' })();
    api.pushEvent({ seq: 1, at: 0, kind: 'run_started', runId: 'r1', scriptId: 's', version: 1, params: {}, startedBy: 'player' });
    expect(bus.all()).toEqual([]);
    expect(api.eventSubscribers()).toBe(0);   // the disposer unsubscribed, it did not just gate
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing filter test**

The predicate is quoted verbatim from the mock at `.dc.html:567-570`; the test is what pins it.

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { matchesFilters } from './eventsViews';

// `amount` is `number | null` with no `?` on ShellEvent, and this literal is annotated
// `: ShellEvent`, so omitting it fails `npm run typecheck` before a case runs.
const ev = (over: Partial<ShellEvent> = {}): ShellEvent => ({
  seq: 1, at: 0, characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'shoth4019zr',
  type: 'xp', skill: 'Woodcutting', text: '+250 Woodcutting xp · 10 actions', tone: 'default', amount: 250, ...over
});
const ALL = { char: 'All', types: [], skill: 'all' } as const;

describe('matchesFilters', () => {
  it('passes everything with the default filters', () => {
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), ALL)).toBe(true);
    expect(matchesFilters(ev({ type: 'bank', skill: null, characterName: null }), ALL)).toBe(true);
  });
  it('filters by character name, single select including All', () => {
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), { ...ALL, char: 'shoth4019zr' })).toBe(true);
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), { ...ALL, char: 'alt_miner' })).toBe(false);
  });
  it('treats an EMPTY type list as all types, not as none', () => {
    // Mutation target: `f.types.includes(e.type)` without the length guard hides every row.
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), { ...ALL, types: [] })).toBe(true);
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), { ...ALL, types: ['loot'] })).toBe(false);
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), { ...ALL, types: ['loot', 'xp'] })).toBe(true);
  });
  it('filters by skill independently of type, and a null skill never matches a named skill', () => {
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), { ...ALL, skill: 'Woodcutting' })).toBe(true);
    expect(matchesFilters(ev({ skill: null }), { ...ALL, skill: 'Woodcutting' })).toBe(false);
    expect(matchesFilters(ev({ skill: null }), { ...ALL, skill: 'all' })).toBe(true);
  });
  it('ANDs the three axes', () => {
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), { char: 'shoth4019zr', types: ['xp'], skill: 'Woodcutting' })).toBe(true);
    expect(matchesFilters(ev(), { char: 'shoth4019zr', types: ['xp'], skill: 'Mining' })).toBe(false);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `eventsViews.ts`**

```ts
export interface EventFilters { char: string; types: ShellEventType[]; skill: string }

/** The mock's own predicate, at .dc.html:567-570. Empty `types` means all types, not none. */
export function matchesFilters(e: ShellEvent, f: EventFilters): boolean {
  return (f.char === 'All' || e.characterName === f.char)
    && (f.types.length === 0 || f.types.includes(e.type))
    && (f.skill === 'all' || e.skill === f.skill);
}

export const EVENT_TYPES: readonly { value: ShellEventType; label: string }[] = [
  { value: 'xp', label: 'XP' }, { value: 'loot', label: 'Loot' }, { value: 'level', label: 'Levels' },
  { value: 'run', label: 'Runs' }, { value: 'claude', label: 'Claude' }, { value: 'bank', label: 'Bank' }
];

const hhmm = (at: number): string => {
  const d = new Date(at);
  return `${String(d.getHours()).padStart(2, '0')}:${String(d.getMinutes()).padStart(2, '0')}`;
};

export function eventRow(e: ShellEvent): HTMLElement {
  const row = h('div', { class: 'events-row', 'data-event-type': e.type, 'data-event-tone': e.tone === 'fail' ? 'fail' : null },
    h('span', { class: 'events-time' }, hhmm(e.at)),
    h('span', { class: 'events-text' }, e.text),
    e.characterName ? h('span', { class: 'badge badge-quiet' }, e.characterName) : null
  );
  return row;
}
```

The rails are pure CSS off `[data-event-type]`, exactly as the trace's are off `[data-kind]`. `layout/events.css`, from the mock's tone table:

```css
.events-row { display: flex; gap: 8px; padding: 6px 6px 6px 8px; margin-bottom: 2px; border-left: 2px solid transparent; border-radius: 0 4px 4px 0; }
.events-row[data-event-type="xp"]     { border-left-color: var(--ok); }
.events-row[data-event-type="loot"]   { border-left-color: var(--warn); }
.events-row[data-event-type="level"]  { border-left-color: var(--accent); background: rgba(255,152,31,.07); }
.events-row[data-event-type="run"]    { border-left-color: var(--info); }
.events-row[data-event-type="claude"] { border-left-color: var(--text-muted); }
.events-row[data-event-type="bank"]   { border-left-color: var(--info-bright); }
.events-row[data-event-tone="fail"]   { border-left-color: var(--error); }
.events-time { flex: none; padding-top: 1px; color: var(--text-faint); font-size: var(--fs-micro); font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; }
.events-text { flex: 1; min-width: 0; color: var(--text); font-size: var(--fs-small); overflow-wrap: anywhere; }
.events-row[data-event-type="level"] .events-text { color: var(--accent-bright); }
.events-row[data-event-tone="fail"] .events-text { color: var(--warn); }
.events-feed { flex: 1; min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto; display: flex; flex-direction: column; }
.events-filters { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 6px; }
.events-chips { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 4px; }
.events-foot { flex: none; display: flex; align-items: center; padding-top: 7px; border-top: 1px solid var(--hairline-soft); color: var(--text-faint); font-size: var(--fs-micro); }
```

The `[data-event-tone="fail"]` rules come **after** the type rules so a failed run row is red-railed and amber-texted, which is the mock's row 10.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the two empty-state patterns (ruling R15)**

Into `alert.css`:

```css
/* Two empty-state patterns, not one. README calls the loot state "the pattern for all empty
   states"; the mock's Events state differs on all eight of its metrics (map-design S16). */
.empty-state { flex: 1; display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; justify-content: center; gap: 9px; padding: 36px 12px; text-align: center; }
.empty-state-mark { display: grid; place-items: center; width: 40px; height: 40px; border: 1.5px dashed var(--text-disabled); border-radius: 50%; color: var(--text-muted); font-size: 16px; animation: bob 2.6s ease-in-out infinite; }
.empty-state-title { color: var(--text-strong); font-size: var(--fs-emph); font-weight: 650; }
.empty-state-copy { max-width: 190px; color: var(--text-muted); font-size: var(--fs-small); }
.empty-state.compact { flex: none; justify-content: flex-start; gap: 7px; padding: 28px 12px; }
.empty-state.compact .empty-state-mark { width: 34px; height: 34px; font-size: 14px; animation: none; }
.empty-state.compact .empty-state-title { font-size: var(--fs-md); }
.empty-state.compact .empty-state-copy { max-width: 180px; }
```

Add `emptyState` to `parts.ts`. It is the only builder in the library with an optional trailing node, so it is spelled out here rather than left to an implementer, exactly as `dot` is in Task 4:

```ts
/**
 * The two empty-state patterns (plan ruling R15). The default is the loot pattern README calls
 * "the pattern for all empty states"; `compact` is the Events one, which differs on all eight of
 * its metrics and, notably, does not bob. `mark` is a Unicode glyph used as data, per the brand
 * guide: the loot state's is `◌` and the Events state's is `⌕`.
 */
export function emptyState(
  mark: string, title: string, copy: string,
  action: HTMLElement | null = null,
  opts: { compact?: boolean } = {}
): HTMLElement {
  return h('div', { class: opts.compact ? 'empty-state compact' : 'empty-state' },
    h('span', { class: 'empty-state-mark', 'aria-hidden': 'true' }, mark),
    h('b', { class: 'empty-state-title' }, title),
    h('span', { class: 'empty-state-copy' }, copy),
    action
  );
}
```

and its test, into `web/src/ui/parts.test.ts`:

```ts
describe('emptyState', () => {
  it('composes mark, title, copy and the action, in that order', () => {
    const btn = h('button', { class: 'btn btn-primary btn-cta' }, 'Run a script');
    const el = emptyState('◌', 'Nothing looted yet', LOOT_EMPTY_COPY, btn);
    expect(el.className).toBe('empty-state');
    expect([...el.children].map(c => c.className))
      .toEqual(['empty-state-mark', 'empty-state-title', 'empty-state-copy', 'btn btn-primary btn-cta']);
    expect(el.querySelector('.empty-state-mark')?.getAttribute('aria-hidden')).toBe('true');
    expect(el.querySelector('.empty-state-copy')?.textContent).toBe(LOOT_EMPTY_COPY);
  });
  it('renders three children and not a stray null when there is no action', () => {
    expect(emptyState('◌', 'Nothing yet', 'copy').children.length).toBe(3);
  });
  it('compact is a modifier on the same element, not a second component', () => {
    // Mutation target: returning a different class name for compact means layout/events.css
    // would need its own empty-state rules, which is the one-off this library exists to prevent.
    const el = emptyState('⌕', 'No events match', EVENTS_EMPTY_COPY, null, { compact: true });
    expect(el.className).toBe('empty-state compact');
    expect(el.querySelector('.empty-state-mark')?.textContent).toBe('⌕');
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the panel**

`builtin/events.ts` composes, top to bottom: the character segmented (`All` plus one option per open character, from `deps.characters()`), the six type chips, the filter row (a `select select-sm` of `All skills` plus the skills present in the log, and a `Clear` `btn btn-outline btn-quiet` carrying `data-events-clear`), the feed, and the footer (`${shown} of ${total} events · this session` plus a `Copy for Claude` `btn btn-link`).

**When the filters match nothing, the feed holds the compact empty state**, not an empty box. The mock specifies it completely (map-design 3.12 item 5) and Task 12 Step 4 has just built it, so leaving it out ships a styled, tested, styleguided component with no caller:

```ts
  function paintFeed(): void {
    const shown = events.filter(e => matchesFilters(e, filters));
    if (shown.length === 0) {
      // The button is grey and 24px, not primary and 27px: the two empty states differ on this
      // as on everything else (ruling R15).
      const clearBtn = h('button', { class: 'btn btn-quiet', type: 'button', onclick: clearFilters }, 'Clear filters');
      feed.replaceChildren(emptyState('⌕', 'No events match', EVENTS_EMPTY_COPY, clearBtn, { compact: true }));
      return;
    }
    // The log is oldest-first (events.ts pushes); the feed is newest-first, as the mock's
    // fourteen rows are.
    feed.replaceChildren(...[...shown].reverse().map(eventRow));
  }
```

`clearFilters` is the one function the filter row's `Clear` and the empty state's `Clear filters` both call: `filters = { char: 'All', types: [], skill: 'all' }`, then repaint. Two buttons, one reset, so they can never drift.

Filters live in the plugin's `panel()` closure, like the Automation tab, so they survive a re-open without a `cs.` key. `mount` renders the current log and subscribes to the bus; `unmount` unsubscribes. It holds no timer at all: the bus pushes.

Appending live is one row, not a rebuild:

```ts
      off = bus.subscribe(e => {
        if (!body || !matchesFilters(e, filters)) { paintFooter(); return; }
        // Newest rows go to the TOP, so the position a reader has to be in to be following the
        // feed is the top, not the bottom. Testing for "at bottom" here would yank a player who
        // had scrolled down through 500 rows back to row 0 on every event, and would leave the
        // player actually watching the live feed alone.
        const following = feed.scrollTop <= 4;
        if (feed.querySelector('.empty-state')) feed.replaceChildren();   // the empty state is gone now
        feed.prepend(eventRow(e));
        if (following) feed.scrollTop = 0;
        paintFooter();
      });
```

`Copy for Claude` writes the filtered rows through the same `UNTRUSTED_HEADER` the trace uses (`builtin/traceView.ts:12`); import it rather than restating it.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the panel test, with a strict fake bus**

```ts
const bus = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
const XP = { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'shoth4019zr', type: 'xp' as const, skill: 'Woodcutting', tone: 'default' as const, amount: 250 };
const LOOT = { characterId: 'c2', characterName: 'alt_miner', type: 'loot' as const, skill: null, tone: 'default' as const, amount: 3 };
const LEVEL = { characterId: 'c1', characterName: 'shoth4019zr', type: 'level' as const, skill: 'Woodcutting', tone: 'default' as const, amount: null };

function seed(): void {
  bus.emit({ ...XP, text: '+250 Woodcutting xp' });
  bus.emit({ ...LOOT, text: 'Picked up 3 iron ore' });
  bus.emit({ ...LEVEL, text: 'Level up! Woodcutting 34 to 35' });
}
const feedText = (body: HTMLElement): (string | null)[] =>
  [...body.querySelectorAll('.events-row .events-text')].map(n => n.textContent);
const foot = (body: HTMLElement): string => body.querySelector('.events-foot')!.textContent ?? '';
const chip = (body: HTMLElement, label: string): HTMLButtonElement =>
  [...body.querySelectorAll<HTMLButtonElement>('.chip')].find(c => c.textContent === label)!;

it('renders only the rows that pass the filters, and the footer counts both', () => {
  seed();
  const body = mount();
  // Newest first, which is the mock's order and the reverse of the bus's own log.
  expect(feedText(body)).toEqual(['Level up! Woodcutting 34 to 35', 'Picked up 3 iron ore', '+250 Woodcutting xp']);
  expect(foot(body)).toContain('3 of 3 events · this session');
});

it('a chip click narrows the feed and updates the footer without a re-mount', () => {
  seed();
  const body = mount();
  const feed = body.querySelector('.events-feed');
  chip(body, 'Loot').click();
  expect(feedText(body)).toEqual(['Picked up 3 iron ore']);
  expect(foot(body)).toContain('1 of 3 events · this session');
  // Mutation target: repainting by remounting the panel replaces this node and loses scroll.
  expect(body.querySelector('.events-feed')).toBe(feed);
  expect(chip(body, 'Loot').getAttribute('aria-pressed')).toBe('true');
});

it('shows the compact empty state when the chips exclude everything, and its button resets all three axes', () => {
  seed();
  const body = mount();
  chip(body, 'Runs').click();                       // no run events in the seed
  const empty = body.querySelector('.events-feed .empty-state.compact')!;
  expect(empty.querySelector('.empty-state-title')?.textContent).toBe('No events match');
  expect(empty.querySelector('.empty-state-copy')?.textContent).toBe(EVENTS_EMPTY_COPY);
  expect(foot(body)).toContain('0 of 3 events · this session');
  empty.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>('button.btn-quiet')!.click();
  expect(feedText(body).length).toBe(3);
  expect([...body.querySelectorAll('.chip')].every(c => c.getAttribute('aria-pressed') === 'false')).toBe(true);
  expect(body.querySelector<HTMLSelectElement>('.select')!.value).toBe('all');
  expect(body.querySelector('.seg-btn.active')?.textContent).toBe('All');
});

it('Clear resets all three axes to All / [] / all', () => {
  seed();
  const body = mount();
  chip(body, 'Loot').click();
  const skill = body.querySelector<HTMLSelectElement>('.select')!;
  skill.value = 'Woodcutting';
  skill.dispatchEvent(new Event('change'));
  expect(feedText(body)).toEqual([]);
  body.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>('[data-events-clear]')!.click();
  expect(feedText(body).length).toBe(3);
  expect(body.querySelector<HTMLSelectElement>('.select')!.value).toBe('all');
  expect([...body.querySelectorAll('.chip')].every(c => c.getAttribute('aria-pressed') === 'false')).toBe(true);
});

it('appends a live event that passes the filters, and counts one that does not', () => {
  seed();
  const body = mount();
  chip(body, 'Loot').click();
  bus.emit({ ...LOOT, text: 'Picked up 1 coal' });
  expect(feedText(body)[0]).toBe('Picked up 1 coal');       // prepended, not appended
  expect(foot(body)).toContain('2 of 4 events · this session');
  bus.emit({ ...XP, text: '+25 Woodcutting xp' });
  expect(feedText(body).length).toBe(2);                     // filtered out of the feed...
  expect(foot(body)).toContain('2 of 5 events · this session');   // ...and still counted
});

it('unmount unsubscribes: nothing renders and nothing throws after it', () => {
  const bus = createEventBus({ now: () => 0 });
  const view = plugin.panel!(ctx);
  view.mount(body);
  view.unmount!();
  bus.emit({ ...base, text: 'after' });
  expect(body.textContent).not.toContain('after');
  expect(() => bus.emit({ ...base, text: 'again' })).not.toThrow();
});
```

### Task 13: The canvas overlays, the HUD stack and the XP drop (G2)

The second consumer of the same xp signal. One producer, two consumers, so a drop and its Events row can never disagree.

Design source: `map-design.md` section 3.4, and `map-contracts.md` section 4.3.

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/src/frame/overlays.ts` (23 lines, rewritten; unchanged by SP4b), `web/src/frame/stage.ts:37` (widen the `StageDeps.overlays` tone union) and `:231` (the pill source), `web/src/sessions/wire.ts:40` (stop writing the hard-coded pill string), `web/src/plugins/builtin/statusHud.ts` (the HUD pills)
- Create: `web/src/frame/overlays.test.ts`. **It does not exist**: `web/src/frame` holds `canvasSize`, `characterTabs`, `overlays.ts`, `panels`, `runBanner`, `stage` and their tests, and `overlays.ts` is the one module there with no test at all today.
- Modify: `web/src/styles/overlay.css` if the wiring needs a class the family lacks

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `pill`, `hudPill` from `parts.ts`; `PairingStore` from Task 11; `EventBus` from Task 11.
- Produces:
  - The whole new signature, because `overlays.ts:1` is `export function createOverlays(root: HTMLElement)` today, with one parameter and no `dispose()`, and the Step 1 tests call `createOverlays(root, deps)` and `ov.dispose()`:

```ts
export interface OverlaysDeps { skillColour(skill: string): string; setTimeout?: typeof setTimeout }
export function createOverlays(root: HTMLElement, deps?: OverlaysDeps): {
  setXpLine(text: string | null): void;
  /**
   * Kept, with the tone union it already has. `overlays.ts:8` is
   * `setStatus(text: string, tone: 'ok' | 'muted' | 'error')`; `frame/stage.ts:37` narrows its
   * own `StageDeps.overlays` copy to `'muted' | 'error'` and calls it at `:225` and `:231-236`,
   * and `sessions/wire.ts:40,45,49` passes `'muted'` and `'error'`. The `'muted' | 'ok' | 'warn'`
   * union this plan first wrote for it does not contain `'error'` and would not have compiled
   * against either caller. Widen `StageDeps`'s copy to the full three rather than narrowing the
   * module, so the two stop disagreeing.
   */
  setStatus(text: string, tone: 'ok' | 'muted' | 'error'): void;
  setPairing(state: PairingState, running: boolean): void;
  dropXp(skill: string, delta: number): void;
  setHidden(hidden: boolean): void;
  dispose(): void;
};
```

  - `setInfoBoxes` and `.ov-box` are **deleted** in Step 4 of this same task, along with the `.ov-boxes` host; the HUD pills replace them here and not in a later task.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing overlay tests**

```ts
describe('pairing pill', () => {
  it('says not paired when unpaired, whatever the run is doing (ruling R6)', () => {
    const ov = createOverlays(root, deps);
    ov.setPairing('unpaired', true);
    const el = root.querySelector('.ov-pairing')!;
    expect(el.textContent).toBe('● not paired');
    expect(el.className).toContain('ov-pill-neutral');
  });
  it('says claude driving only when paired AND running', () => {
    const ov = createOverlays(root, deps);
    ov.setPairing('paired-live', true);
    expect(root.querySelector('.ov-pairing')!.textContent).toBe('● claude driving');
    ov.setPairing('paired-live', false);
    expect(root.querySelector('.ov-pairing')!.textContent).toBe('● claude paired');
    expect(root.querySelector('.ov-pairing')!.className).toContain('ov-pill-ok');
  });
  it('treats a stale pairing as paired: the session exists, it is just quiet', () => {
    const ov = createOverlays(root, deps);
    ov.setPairing('paired-stale', false);
    expect(root.querySelector('.ov-pairing')!.textContent).toBe('● claude paired');
  });
});

describe('dropXp', () => {
  it('mounts a drop with the skill colour and an outlined +N', () => {
    const ov = createOverlays(root, deps);
    ov.dropXp('Woodcutting', 25);
    const drop = root.querySelector('.xp-drop')!;
    expect(drop.querySelector('.xp-drop-value')?.textContent).toBe('+25');
    expect((drop.querySelector('.xp-drop-chip') as HTMLElement).style.background).not.toBe('');
  });
  it('removes the drop when its animation ends, and leaves nothing behind', () => {
    const ov = createOverlays(root, deps);
    ov.dropXp('Woodcutting', 25);
    root.querySelector('.xp-drop')!.dispatchEvent(new Event('animationend'));
    expect(root.querySelector('.xp-drop')).toBeNull();
  });
  it('caps concurrent drops so a burst cannot fill the canvas', () => {
    const ov = createOverlays(root, deps);
    for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) ov.dropXp('Woodcutting', 25);
    expect(root.querySelectorAll('.xp-drop').length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(4);
  });
  it('fires nothing after dispose', () => {
    const ov = createOverlays(root, deps);
    ov.dispose();
    ov.dropXp('Woodcutting', 25);
    expect(root.querySelector('.xp-drop')).toBeNull();
  });
});
```

jsdom fires no real `animationend`, which is why the removal is driven by the event and the test dispatches it by hand; a `setTimeout` fallback of 2600ms is armed alongside and cleared by whichever fires first, so a browser tab that was backgrounded during the animation still cleans up. Assert that fallback too, with `vi.useFakeTimers()`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Rewrite `overlays.ts`**

Build with `h()` rather than the current `innerHTML` string, so the pills are the library's:

```ts
export function createOverlays(root: HTMLElement, deps: { skillColour(skill: string): string; setTimeout?: typeof setTimeout } = defaults) {
  const xpLine = pill('', 'accent', { corner: true });
  xpLine.classList.add('ov-xp', 'hidden');
  const pairing = pill('● not paired', 'neutral', { corner: true });
  pairing.classList.add('ov-pairing');
  const hud = h('div', { class: 'hud-stack' });
  root.replaceChildren(xpLine, pairing, hud);
  ...
}
```

`dropXp` builds the drop, appends it, and arms both an `animationend` listener and a timer; `MAX_DROPS = 4` and the oldest is removed when a fifth arrives. The chip colour comes from `deps.skillColour(skill)`; the mock hard-codes `#2e7d32` for Woodcutting (note S10), so the default implementation is a `SKILL_COLOURS` map in `web/src/stats/skills.ts` with `--xp-chip` as the fallback for a skill with no colour.

- [ ] **Step 3: Wire the sources**

`sessions/wire.ts:40` writes the literal `'● not paired'` on every login and `frame/stage.ts:231` writes the same string on every tab switch. Neither is derived from anything (ruling R6 and `map-contracts.md` 8.8). Delete both writes; `main.ts` subscribes the overlay's `setPairing` to the pairing store and to the run status, and `stage.syncChrome` stops touching the pill.

The xp drop subscribes to the bus and draws only for the **active** character, because the drop is over the active canvas:

```ts
  bus.subscribe(e => {
    if (e.type !== 'xp' || e.characterId !== stage.sessions.active()?.id) return;
    overlays.dropXp(e.skill ?? '', e.amount ?? 0);
  });
```

Do not parse the text: `ShellEvent.amount` is the field Task 11 declares for exactly this, set by `xpProducer` from `ev.delta` and by `lootProducer` from the added count. Read `e.amount ?? 0` and drop the regex.

Replace with `hudPill('hp' | 'prayer' | 'run', pct, value)` into the `.hud-stack`, plus the boost pill (`pill('Woodcutting +3', 'accent')`) when `showBoosts` is on. `setInfoBoxes` and `.ov-box` are then unused; delete both and the `.ov-boxes` host.

### Task 14: The co-pilot bar (G8.1)

The persistent 42px bar above the canvas, in all five states, replacing the run banner that only existed during a run. It reads **two** sources, not one: the run state from the tasks router, and the pairing state from Task 11's singleton. It also becomes the one home for the run-state vocabulary (ruling R22), which two modules currently keep divergent copies of.

Design source: `map-design.md` section 3.3 (every literal, the five-state dot/rule matrix, all five bodies) and `map-contracts.md` section 1 in full.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/frame/runPresentation.ts`, `web/src/frame/runPresentation.test.ts`, `web/src/frame/copilotBar.ts`, `web/src/frame/copilotBar.test.ts`, `web/src/styles/layout/copilot.css`
- Delete: `web/src/frame/runBanner.ts` (**302 lines at HEAD, not the 160 this plan was written against**), `web/src/frame/runBanner.test.ts` (254 lines)
- Modify: `web/src/partials/frame.html:12`, `web/src/main.ts:9,79,119-120,258`, `web/src/plugins/builtin/tasksViews.ts:31-48` (`statusLabel` consumes `presentRun`), `web/src/styles/index.css`, `web/styleguide.html:468-478` (the `#run-banner` section becomes `#copilot`), **`web/src/styleguide.ts:3,82-88`** (it imports `createRunBanner` and drives the four `[data-sg-banner]` demos with the real renderer, so deleting the module without editing this file fails `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck`; this plan named neither file before the reconcile pass), `web/src/frame/stage.ts:191` (a comment that names `frame/runBanner.ts` as the panic key's home) and `:291` (a comment that says "the run banner")
- Test: `web/e2e/tasks.pw.test.ts:90,92,99,106,107,108,111,112,118,119,128,129,130,141,144,171,174,181,227`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `RunStatus`, `RunState`, `PauseReason`, `HealthCondition` from `web/src/tasks/types.ts`; `PairingState` from `web/src/frame/pairing.ts`; `SessionStatus` from `web/src/sessions/types.ts`; `dot`, `pill` from `parts.ts`; `BAR_UNPAIRED`, `BAR_PAUSED` from `copy.ts`.
- Produces:

```ts
// web/src/frame/runPresentation.ts
export type CopilotState = 'unpaired' | 'standby' | 'running' | 'paused' | 'stuck';
export interface RunPresentationInput {
  status: RunStatus;
  pairing: PairingState;
  session: SessionStatus;
  now: number;
  /**
   * When the bar first saw this run in a terminal state, or null. `RunStatus` carries no such
   * stamp and `startedAt` is not one: a run that ran for ten seconds and then failed is already
   * past `startedAt + lingerMs`, so keying the linger off `startedAt` means a failure never shows
   * the stuck chrome at all. The bar stamps this in its own closure the first tick it sees a
   * terminal state, exactly as `runBanner.ts:34,37` does today with `SETTLED`.
   */
  settledAt: number | null;
  /** Settled runs linger this long after `settledAt` before the bar gets out of the way. */
  lingerMs?: number;
}
export interface RunPresentation {
  state: CopilotState;
  /** The bar's dot and rule, and the strip corner dot. */
  tone: 'idle' | 'ok' | 'accent' | 'warn' | 'error';
  headline: string;
  detail: string | null;
  /** Seconds to auto-resume, or null. */
  resumeIn: number | null;
  /** True while `session` is not online: the primary action is disabled and the copy is moved. */
  blocked: boolean;
  /** What the canvas pairing pill should read. Derived from pairing FIRST (ruling R6). */
  pill: '● not paired' | '● claude paired' | '● claude driving';
}
export function presentRun(input: RunPresentationInput): RunPresentation;
export const HEALTH_COPY: Record<HealthCondition, string>;
```

```ts
// web/src/frame/copilotBar.ts
// `bar`, `detail` and `rule` are three SIBLINGS, not a parent and two children. The rule is a
// decorative animated 2px band, the detail row changes every second, and `#copilot-bar` is
// `role="status"`, so putting either inside the live region would make it part of what a screen
// reader announces. The bar is also a fixed 42px single row and cannot hold a second line the
// way the banner could. Step 6's frame.html writes all three as siblings and Step 4's tests
// therefore query `.copilot-rule` and `.copilot-detail` off the parent, never off `bar`.
export interface CopilotBarDeps { bar: HTMLElement; detail: HTMLElement; rule: HTMLElement; strip(): HTMLElement; api(): TasksApi; pairing(): PairingState; session(): SessionStatus; openPanel(id: PanelId): void; openTrace(): void; notify(message: string, kind?: 'info' | 'error'): void; now?(): number }
export interface CopilotBar { update(status: RunStatus): void; refresh(): void; dispose(): void }
export function createCopilotBar(deps: CopilotBarDeps): CopilotBar;
```

**This task deletes the file SP4b Task 10 spent most of its effort in, so every behaviour it added is listed here and each one has a home on the bar.** `runBanner.ts` doubled under SP4b, from about 160 lines to 302. Nine behaviours came with that growth and none of them is in the design brief, the companion or the mock, so nothing else in this plan would have asked for them back. Re-earn all nine, and let `copilotBar.test.ts` carry the case that proves each one, moved from `runBanner.test.ts`:

| SP4b behaviour | Where it is now | Where it goes on the bar | The case that proves it |
|---|---|---|---|
| The detail line: a second row naming the target, the position and the xp rate | `runBanner.ts:125` (`detailEl`, `data-banner-detail`), built by `detailParts` at `:178-186` | `#copilot-detail`, a **sibling** of `#copilot-bar` (Step 5 says why), split into `[data-bar-detail]` and `[data-bar-rate]` | `runBanner.test.ts:164-180` |
| It is `aria-hidden="true"`, because the bar is an atomic `role="status"` and the line moves every second | `:125` | unchanged, on the new node | `runBanner.test.ts:172` |
| It takes itself off screen when it has nothing to say, and the root loses `has-detail` with it | `:190-194` | the row's own `hidden` attribute alone. The `has-detail` class and the four pill offsets it drove go away: Step 6 moves the bar out of `#canvas-wrap`, so nothing is underneath it to push down | `runBanner.test.ts:173-179`, `:182-188` |
| The collapse toggle, a `▾` button with `aria-expanded` and a title that says what it does | `:126-130` (`data-banner-detail-toggle`) | hook `data-bar-detail-toggle`, same markup | `runBanner.test.ts:202-219` |
| The collapsed flag lives in the closure and **nothing persists it** (SP4b spec decision 12) | `:105`, reset by `dispose()` at `:296` | unchanged; ruling R27 says the same thing for every other piece of UI state in this plan | `runBanner.test.ts:213-216` |
| The health pip, an amber dot lit only while a recovery is running, titled with the condition | `:113` (`data-banner-health`), painted at `:199-201` | hook `data-bar-health`, beside the dot | `runBanner.test.ts:190-200` |
| `detailParts` reads the position off `api().getState()?.player`, because `RunStatus` carries none, and a missing player is the ordinary pre-login state and not an error | `:182-183` | unchanged; `CopilotBarDeps.api()` is already there | `runBanner.test.ts:165,169` |
| The detail line is painted from the **status**, never from the clock, so a per-second tick does not rewrite it | `paintDetail(s)` is called from `paint()` at `:234` with the status, and `setText` at `:205-207` guards the write | unchanged | `runBanner.test.ts:153-162` |
| `dispose()` takes the detail line, the pip and the `has-detail` class with it | `:296-299` | unchanged | `runBanner.test.ts:221-228` |

`RunStatus.target`, `.health` and `.xpPerHour` (`tasks/types.ts:309`, `:311`, `:313`) are all on HEAD, so none of this is conditional and the `// SP4b Task 10:` marker the executor notes offered as a fallback is not needed.

**The bar also keeps a slot for a rate-derived line, which is a door sprint 3 asked for and this task pays for now.** The sprint control board records it as "its co-pilot bar keeps a slot for a rate-derived line". `detailParts` at `:184` already emits one entry per skill out of `s.xpPerHour`, so the slot exists as data; what it needs is a place in the bar's structure that a later entry can fill without moving anything. Give the detail row two children rather than one string:

```ts
// `.copilot-detail` is a row of two spans, not one line of joined text. The left one is what the
// run is doing (target, position, recovery) and the right one is what it is EARNING. Sprint 3's
// Sound entry and any later "time to level" line write into the right one; keeping them apart
// means such a line is a text write into a node that already exists, and not a re-layout of the
// bar in a task that is about something else.
const detailWhat = h('span', { class: 'copilot-detail-what', 'data-bar-detail': '' });
const detailRate = h('span', { class: 'copilot-detail-rate num', 'data-bar-rate': '' });
```

`detailWhat` takes the target, the recovery note and the position, joined by `·` exactly as `detailParts` joins them today. `detailRate` takes the `xpPerHour` entries alone. Both are `aria-hidden`, both are inside `.copilot-detail`, and `paintDetail` hides the row when **both** are empty. Add a `copilotBar.test.ts` case asserting that a status with `xpPerHour` and no target fills `[data-bar-rate]`, leaves `[data-bar-detail]` empty, and still shows the row.

The companion asks for the five presented states to be checked against the runtime's literals before anything is built on them. `map-contracts.md` section 1 has already run that check; this step is to confirm it still holds after SP4b, not to redo it. Run these three and record the output in the task's review note:

```bash
grep -n "export type RunState\|export type PauseReason" web/src/tasks/types.ts
grep -n "export type HealthCondition\|export type FailReason" web/src/tasks/types.ts
grep -rn "state: 'paused', reason:" web/src/tasks/runner.ts
```

Expected, and what the plan is written against:
- `RunState` has **seven** values and `stuck` is **not** one of them: `idle | starting | running | paused | done | failed | stopped`.
- `stuck` is a `PauseReason`, set together with `state: 'paused'` at `runner.ts:118`; `hard-stop` is set the same way at `:131`.
- `HealthCondition` has nine values, `FailReason` thirteen, and **neither ever reaches the bar as a state**: they are copy for the stuck row's second line.
- The companion's "eleven state literals" (section 2 row 4 and G8.1) is wrong: `died`, `disconnected` and `unreachable` are `FailReason` values, not health-ladder conditions, and the mapping is over three axes, not one.

All four were re-run at HEAD on 2026-09-07 and all four hold: `RunState` is `tasks/types.ts:243`, `PauseReason` is `:242`, `HealthCondition` is `:104-106` (nine), `FailReason` is `:114-119` (thirteen), and the two `set({ state: 'paused', reason: ... })` calls are `runner.ts:118` and `:131`. Re-run them anyway; if any of it has changed, stop and re-derive the table in Step 2 before writing code.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing presentation test**

The whole mapping table, so nothing falls through. Rows are `map-contracts.md` section 1.4.

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { presentRun } from './runPresentation';

// `RunStatus.statusLine` is `string` and not `string | null` (tasks/types.ts:281), so a fixture
// with `statusLine: null` fails `npm run typecheck` for a reason unrelated to its subject. The
// empty string is the "no line" value, and presentRun's second arm reads `statusLine.trim() ||
// null` so it falls through to the fallback rather than rendering blank.
// `target`, `health` and `xpPerHour` are SP4b Task 10's fields, confirmed on HEAD at
// tasks/types.ts:309, :311 and :313. All three are REQUIRED on RunStatus (no `?`), so a fixture
// that omits any of them fails `npm run typecheck` before a case runs.
const status = (over: Partial<RunStatus> = {}): RunStatus => ({
  state: 'idle', reason: null, runId: null, scriptId: null, scriptName: null, task: null,
  statusLine: '', attempts: 0, startedAt: null, attached: false, resumeAtMs: null,
  target: null, health: null, xpPerHour: {}, ...over
});
const at = (over: Partial<RunPresentationInput> = {}): RunPresentationInput =>
  ({ status: status(), pairing: 'paired-live', session: 'online', now: 10_000, settledAt: null, ...over });

describe('presentRun', () => {
  it('maps every RunState onto one of the five presented states, with nothing falling through', () => {
    const seen = new Set<CopilotState>();
    for (const state of ['idle', 'starting', 'running', 'paused', 'done', 'failed', 'stopped'] as const) {
      // settledAt inside the linger window is what lets `failed` produce `stuck` here; with the
      // default null there is no anchor and this set would be missing it.
      seen.add(presentRun(at({ status: status({ state }), settledAt: 10_000 })).state);
    }
    expect([...seen].sort()).toEqual(['paused', 'running', 'standby', 'stuck']);
  });

  it('reads paused+stuck as stuck and paused+hard-stop as stuck', () => {
    expect(presentRun(at({ status: status({ state: 'paused', reason: 'stuck' }) })).state).toBe('stuck');
    expect(presentRun(at({ status: status({ state: 'paused', reason: 'hard-stop' }) })).state).toBe('stuck');
  });

  it('reads every other pause reason, and a null reason, as paused', () => {
    for (const reason of ['player', 'claude', 'human-input', null] as const) {
      expect(presentRun(at({ status: status({ state: 'paused', reason }) })).state, String(reason)).toBe('paused');
    }
  });

  it('reads starting as running: the run exists the moment it is asked for', () => {
    expect(presentRun(at({ status: status({ state: 'starting' }) })).state).toBe('running');
  });

  it('lingers a failed run in the stuck chrome from when it SETTLED, then lets it go (ruling R4)', () => {
    // startedAt is deliberately old: a run that ran for ten seconds and then failed must still
    // get its full linger window. Keying off startedAt would give it none.
    const s = status({ state: 'failed', startedAt: -10_000 });
    expect(presentRun(at({ status: s, settledAt: 0, now: 1_000, lingerMs: 4_000 })).state).toBe('stuck');
    expect(presentRun(at({ status: s, settledAt: 0, now: 9_000, lingerMs: 4_000 })).state).toBe('standby');
  });

  it('does not linger done or stopped: that news belongs in the toast', () => {
    for (const state of ['done', 'stopped'] as const) {
      expect(presentRun(at({ status: status({ state }), settledAt: 10_000 })).state).toBe('standby');
    }
  });

  it('is unpaired only when the account is unpaired AND no run is live (ruling R6)', () => {
    expect(presentRun(at({ pairing: 'unpaired' })).state).toBe('unpaired');
    expect(presentRun(at({ pairing: 'unpaired', status: status({ state: 'running' }) })).state).toBe('running');
    // ...and the pill still tells the truth about pairing.
    expect(presentRun(at({ pairing: 'unpaired', status: status({ state: 'running' }) })).pill).toBe('● not paired');
    expect(presentRun(at({ pairing: 'paired-live', status: status({ state: 'running' }) })).pill).toBe('● claude driving');
    expect(presentRun(at({ pairing: 'paired-stale' })).pill).toBe('● claude paired');
  });

  it('blocks the primary action while the character is not online, with the moved copy (ruling R5)', () => {
    const p = presentRun(at({ session: 'connecting' }));
    expect(p.blocked).toBe(true);
    // The four literals are MOVED from stage.ts:231-235, byte for byte. The ellipsis is U+2026
    // and the separator is U+00B7; do not "tidy" either into ASCII, and do not retype them.
    expect(p.headline).toBe('connecting\u2026');
    expect(presentRun(at({ session: 'booting' })).headline).toBe('starting\u2026');
    expect(presentRun(at({ session: 'offline' })).headline).toBe('offline \u00b7 press Login to reconnect');
    expect(presentRun(at({ session: 'title' })).headline).toBe('press Login to play');
  });

  it('takes the stuck reason from the health condition, then statusLine, then a fallback (ruling R23)', () => {
    const stuck = (over: Partial<RunStatus>) => presentRun(at({ status: status({ state: 'paused', reason: 'stuck', task: 'Chop tree', ...over }) }));
    expect(stuck({ health: { condition: 'no-progress', since: 0 } } as Partial<RunStatus>).detail).toBe('nothing has moved for a while');
    expect(stuck({ statusLine: 'chopping the nearest tree' }).detail).toBe('chopping the nearest tree');
    expect(stuck({ statusLine: '   ' }).detail).toBe('no reason reported');   // blank is not a reason
    expect(stuck({}).detail).toBe('no reason reported');
    expect(stuck({}).headline).toBe('stuck on Chop tree');
  });

  it('counts the resume down in whole seconds and never below zero', () => {
    const p = (resumeAtMs: number, now: number) => presentRun(at({ status: status({ state: 'paused', reason: 'player', resumeAtMs }), now })).resumeIn;
    expect(p(15_000, 10_000)).toBe(5);
    expect(p(10_400, 10_000)).toBe(1);      // rounds up: "resumes in 0s" is never shown
    expect(p(9_000, 10_000)).toBe(0);
  });
});
```

`RunStatus.health` is on HEAD (`tasks/types.ts:311`), so the `health` arm compiles as written and the fallback this step used to describe is no longer needed. `HEALTH_COPY` is a `Record<HealthCondition, string>` over the nine conditions at `types.ts:104-106`, which is what makes a tenth condition a compile error here.

```ts
export const HEALTH_COPY: Record<HealthCondition, string> = {
  'no-progress': 'nothing has moved for a while',
  'unexpected-interface': 'an interface opened that the script did not expect',
  'dialog-stuck': 'a dialogue is waiting for an answer',
  'level-up': 'a level-up interrupted the script',
  death: 'the character died',
  logout: 'the character was logged out',
  'inventory-full': 'the inventory is full',
  'out-of-supplies': 'the supplies ran out',
  'low-hp': 'hp fell below the hard stop'
};
```

Everything `runBanner.test.ts` pinned, re-earned (ruling R25), plus the five states.

```ts
describe('the co-pilot bar', () => {
  it('is a polite atomic status region, and the ticking parts are hidden from it', () => {
    const bar = mount();
    expect(root.getAttribute('role')).toBe('status');
    expect(root.querySelector('[data-bar-elapsed]')?.getAttribute('aria-hidden')).toBe('true');
    expect(root.querySelector('[data-bar-countdown]')?.getAttribute('aria-hidden')).toBe('true');
  });

  it('mutates the elapsed node in place, so a tick does not re-announce the bar', () => {
    const bar = mount();
    bar.update(running({ startedAt: 0 }));
    const first = root.querySelector('[data-bar-elapsed]');
    clock += 1000; bar.update(running({ startedAt: 0 }));
    // Mutation target: rebuilding the bar on every tick replaces this node and fails here.
    expect(root.querySelector('[data-bar-elapsed]')).toBe(first);
    expect(first!.textContent).toBe('00:01');
  });

  it('renders all five states with the mock copy', () => {
    const bar = mount();
    bar.update(status({ state: 'idle' })); pairing = 'unpaired'; bar.refresh();
    expect(root.textContent).toContain('not paired — Claude can play this character alongside you');
    expect(root.querySelector('button.btn-primary')?.textContent).toBe('Pair Claude');
    pairing = 'paired-live'; bar.refresh();
    expect(root.textContent).toContain('paired · standing by');
    expect(root.querySelector('button.btn-primary')?.textContent).toBe('Run a script');
    bar.update(running({ scriptName: 'Chop and drop', task: 'Chop tree', statusLine: 'chopping the nearest tree · 41 logs' }));
    expect(root.textContent).toContain('Chop and drop');
    expect(root.querySelector('kbd')?.textContent).toBe('Esc');
    expect([...root.querySelectorAll('.btn')].map(b => b.textContent)).toEqual(['Pause', 'Stop']);
    bar.update(status({ state: 'paused', reason: 'player', resumeAtMs: 15_000, startedAt: 0 }));
    expect(root.textContent).toContain('paused — you took control');
    expect(root.textContent).toContain('resumes in 5s');
    bar.update(status({ state: 'paused', reason: 'stuck', task: 'Chop tree', startedAt: 0 }));
    expect(root.textContent).toContain('stuck on Chop tree');
    expect([...root.querySelectorAll('.btn')].map(b => b.textContent)).toEqual(['Open trace', 'Stop']);
    expect(root.querySelector('.btn-danger')?.textContent).toBe('Stop');
  });

  it('animates the rule only while running, on the sibling node it was handed', () => {
    const bar = mount();   // mount() builds `{ bar: root, rule }` and hands both to createCopilotBar
    bar.update(running({}));
    expect(rule.getAttribute('data-state')).toBe('running');
    bar.update(status({ state: 'paused', reason: 'stuck' }));
    expect(rule.getAttribute('data-state')).toBe('stuck');
    // Mutation target: `root.querySelector('.copilot-rule')` finds nothing, because the rule is
    // a sibling of the status region and not inside it.
    expect(root.querySelector('.copilot-rule')).toBeNull();
  });

  it('draws the unpaired dot at the mock\'s lighter idle grey', () => {
    const bar = mount();
    pairing = 'unpaired'; bar.update(status({ state: 'idle' }));
    // map-design 3.3: the unpaired bar dot is #666 (--dot-idle), not the family default
    // --text-faint. `dot('idle', { explicit: true })` is what emits it (Task 4 Step 5).
    expect(root.querySelector('.dot')?.className).toContain('dot-idle');
  });

  it('paints the strip dot onto the Automation button and clears it when the run settles', () => {
    const bar = mount();
    bar.update(running({}));
    const dotEl = strip.querySelector('[data-panel="tasks"] .strip-dot')!;
    expect(dotEl.className).toContain('dot-accent');
    bar.update(status({ state: 'done' }));
    expect(strip.querySelector('[data-panel="tasks"] .strip-dot')).toBeNull();
  });

  it('Escape pauses a running run, and only a running run', () => {
    const bar = mount();
    bar.update(running({}));
    document.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: 'Escape' }));
    expect(api.pause).toHaveBeenCalledWith('player');
    api.pause.mockClear();
    bar.update(status({ state: 'paused', reason: 'player' }));
    document.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: 'Escape' }));
    expect(api.pause).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it('announces a terminal outcome once, however often the router republishes it', () => {
    const bar = mount();
    const done = status({ state: 'done', runId: 'r1' });
    bar.update(done); bar.update(done); bar.update(done);
    expect(notify).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
  });

  it('fires nothing after dispose: no timer, no key handler, no strip paint', () => {
    const bar = mount();
    bar.update(running({}));
    bar.dispose();
    clock += 5000;
    document.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: 'Escape' }));
    expect(api.pause).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
    expect(strip.querySelector('.strip-dot')).toBeNull();
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `copilotBar.ts`**

Lift `runBanner.ts`'s working parts wholesale, at their HEAD line numbers: `mmss` (`:41-46`), `secondsToResume` (`:65-68`), `signature` (`:74-76`), `setText` (`:205-207`), `paintDot` (`:149-159`), the one `TICK_MS = 1000` interval (`:36`, driven by `tick()` at `:246-249`), the `announced` dedupe set keyed `runId:state:reason` with its `ANNOUNCE_MEMORY = 32` cap (`:39`, `:97`, `:274-282`), `announce()` (`:251-258`), the `document` keydown handler that only ever calls `api.pause('player')` while running (`:260-265`), and the `dispose()` that removes it (`:288-300`). Take `paintDetail` (`:189-202`) and `detailParts` (`:178-186`) with them, per the carry-forward table above.

`runBanner.ts:34`'s `LINGER_MS = 4000` and `:37`'s `SETTLED` set become the closure's `settledAt` stamp, which is what `presentRun` needs for R4: on every `update`, if the state is terminal and `settledAt` is null, stamp `now()`; if it is not terminal, clear it. The `linger` timeout at `:98` and `:284` and the `lingered` flag at `:89` go with them, because the bar never hides and has nothing to schedule; `LINGER_MS` moves inside `presentRun` as its `lingerMs` default.

`paintDot` is lifted as it stands except for one class: `runBanner.ts:157` emits `dot-live`, which Task 4 retired in favour of `dot-pulse`, so it is already `dot-pulse` by the time this task runs. `tasks.css:172`'s reduced-motion rule names `.strip-dot.dot-live` and moved to `strip.css` under Task 2; it takes the same rename. Replace `message()` and `tone()` with `presentRun`. The bar is always visible: drop `.hidden` and the `SETTLED` visibility logic, keep `LINGER_MS` inside `presentRun`.

Structure, from the mock: a `.copilot-bar` row (`height: var(--bar-h)`, `gap:10px`, `padding:0 14px`, `background: linear-gradient(180deg,#202022,#1c1c1e)`), holding the 9px dot, the health pip beside it, the constant `Claude` label (note S4: it is in **all five** states and README never mentions it), the state body, a `<span class="grow">` spacer, the mono elapsed (14px, `--accent-bright` running / `--warn` paused / `--text-muted` stuck; note S5: the mock shows it in paused and stuck too, where README lists it only under running), the detail toggle, then the buttons. Beneath it the `.copilot-detail` row, and beneath that a 2px `.copilot-rule` carrying `data-state`.

**The detail row is a sibling of `#copilot-bar`, not a child, and that is a change from how the banner did it.** `runBanner.ts:146` puts `detailEl` inside `#run-banner` and lets it wrap onto a second line, which works because the banner has no fixed height. The bar does: the mock draws it at exactly 42px, one row, in all five states, so a wrapped child would either be clipped or make the bar 60px and stop being the surface the design specifies. So `frame.html` writes three siblings, `#copilot-bar`, `.copilot-detail` and `.copilot-rule`, and `createCopilotBar` is handed all three the way Step 6 hands it the rule.

Moving it out of `role="status"` is a **strengthening** of ruling R25 and not a weakening: the reason SP4b marked it `aria-hidden="true"` was that a line changing every second inside an atomic live region is re-announced every second, and a sibling is not in the region at all. **Keep the `aria-hidden="true"` anyway**, on both spans, so that a later change which moves the row back inside cannot silently make it announceable. The elapsed and countdown nodes stay inside the bar with theirs, exactly as R25 requires.

`layout/copilot.css` from `map-design.md` section 3.3's table:

```css
.copilot-bar { height: var(--bar-h); flex: none; display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 10px; padding: 0 14px; background: linear-gradient(180deg,#202022,#1c1c1e); }
.copilot-label { color: var(--text-strong); font-weight: 650; letter-spacing: .01em; }
.copilot-text { min-width: 0; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap; color: var(--text-muted); }
.copilot-summary { min-width: 0; display: flex; align-items: baseline; gap: 6px; overflow: hidden; white-space: nowrap; padding: 0; border: 0; background: none; color: var(--text); text-align: left; font-size: var(--fs-base); cursor: pointer; }
.copilot-summary:hover { color: var(--text-strong); }
.copilot-esc { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 6px; color: var(--text-muted); font-size: var(--fs-small); }
.copilot-elapsed { font-family: var(--font-num); font-weight: 700; font-size: 14px; font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; color: var(--accent-bright); }
/* The second row (SP4b Task 10's detail line, re-earned; see this task's carry-forward table).
   `.copilot-detail-what` is what the run is doing and `.copilot-detail-rate` is what it is
   earning; the rate span is the reserved slot a later entry writes into. */
.copilot-detail { flex: none; display: flex; align-items: baseline; gap: 8px; min-width: 0; padding: 0 14px 3px; background: #1c1c1e; color: var(--text-muted); font-size: var(--fs-small); }
.copilot-detail[hidden] { display: none; }
.copilot-detail-what { min-width: 0; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap; }
.copilot-detail-rate { margin-left: auto; flex: none; font-family: var(--font-num); font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; color: var(--ok-pop); }
.copilot-detail-toggle { flex: none; padding: 0 2px; border: 0; background: none; color: var(--text-muted); font: inherit; line-height: 1; cursor: pointer; }
.copilot-detail-toggle:hover { color: var(--text-strong); }
.copilot-detail-toggle[aria-expanded="false"] { transform: rotate(-90deg); }
/* The bar's own health pip: amber, because a run recovering is not a run working. */
.copilot-health { flex: none; background: var(--warn); box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px rgba(255,179,0,.25); }
.copilot-rule { height: 2px; background: rgba(0,0,0,.5); background-size: 200% 100%; }
.copilot-rule[data-state="running"] { background-image: linear-gradient(90deg,#d6780a,#ffb95e,#d6780a); animation: barflow 2.6s linear infinite; }
.copilot-rule[data-state="paused"] { background: var(--warn); }
.copilot-rule[data-state="stuck"] { background: var(--error); }
.copilot-bar[data-state="paused"] .copilot-elapsed { color: var(--warn); }
.copilot-bar[data-state="stuck"] .copilot-elapsed { color: var(--text-muted); }
```

`builtin/tasksViews.ts:31-48` `statusLabel(s, now)` becomes a thin adapter over `presentRun`, returning the badge text and tone the run card wants from the same five states. Its existing tests keep their assertions where the two agreed and are updated where they did not; record each changed string in the task's review note, because a copy change on the run card is visible to `tasks.pw`.

**Two consumers, and a third that is deliberately left alone.** `statusLabel` is called from `renderRunCard` (`tasksViews.ts:109`) and `updateRunCard` (`:149`), and both are in scope here. `runReportView.ts:22`'s `STATUS_TONE` is the third copy of the vocabulary at HEAD and is **not** in scope: it maps `RunSummary.status` (a finished run's outcome, never `running` or `paused`) onto a badge tone in the report header, so it has three live values against `presentRun`'s five states, and folding it in would make `presentRun` answer a question about history rather than about the bar. Say so in the review note, so a reviewer does not read the omission as an oversight.

**The four strings `tasks.pw` asserts through the run card, which Step 8 has to move with this one.** `statusLabel` today returns `'Running'`, `'Paused'`, `'Paused: you took control - resumes in Ns'` and `'Stuck on <task>'`; `tasks.pw.test.ts:99`, `:108`, `:112` and `:130` assert the first two by exact text through `[data-run-card] [data-run-state]`. Decide the run card's wording in this step, then update those four assertions in Step 8 to whatever it is. The bar's own copy is the mock's, sentence case and carrying the mock's em dash under ruling R1; the run card's badge is a **badge**, so title case is right there, and the two are allowed to differ in case while agreeing in content. What they may not do is disagree in content, which is what R22 exists to stop.

### Task 15: The Automation panel surfaces

The run card is patched in place, not rebuilt: the README says so and roughly 24 test references depend on its hooks (`[data-run-card]`, `[data-run-state]`, `[data-run-pause]`, `[data-run-resume]`, `[data-run-stop]`, `[data-run-task]`, `[data-run-restart]`). Everything around it takes the v2 shapes.

Design source: `map-design.md` section 3.9, and notes S14 (elapsed is 16px), S17 (history meta is not mono), S18 (the card buttons are `sm`, not `xs`), S23 and ruling R19 (the `⚠` goes).

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/src/plugins/builtin/tasksViews.ts` (299 lines at HEAD), `builtin/tasks.ts` and `builtin/tasksHistory.ts` (Task 10 Step 0b split them), `builtin/marketplace.ts`, `builtin/runReportView.ts` (the `.report*` surfaces, restyled but not rewritten), `web/src/styles/layout/automation.css`, `web/styleguide.html`
- Test: `web/src/plugins/builtin/tasksViews.test.ts` (320 lines, and the sixteen-site signature migration lands in it), `tasks.test.ts`, `tasks.market.test.ts`, `marketplace.test.ts`, `runReportView.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `card`, `sectionLabel`, `tag`, `dot`, `meter`, `segmented` from `parts.ts`; `badge`, `alert` from `el.ts`; `presentRun` from Task 14.
- Produces: no new module, but **two changed signatures**, because the tests below call both functions with arguments they do not have. **SP4b Task 10 gave both a fourth positional parameter and the plan's first draft of this block silently dropped it.** At HEAD, `tasksViews.ts:108` is `renderRunCard(status: RunStatus, handlers: Partial<RunHandlers>, now: number = Date.now(), notes: HealthNote[] = [])` and `:148` is `updateRunCard(card: HTMLElement, status: RunStatus, now: number = Date.now(), notes: HealthNote[] = [])`. `notes` is the run card's last-five health checks; it feeds `notesList` (`:137-141`) and `fillNotes` (`:86-100`), and dropping it deletes SP4b Task 10's work in the run card the same way replacing `runBanner.ts` would have deleted it in the banner. It moves onto the options object rather than out:

```ts
export interface RunStep { label: string; state: 'done' | 'current' | 'upcoming' }
export interface RunCounters { logs: number; sessionXp: number }
export interface RunCardDeps {
  now?: number;
  steps?: readonly RunStep[];
  /** SP4b Task 10's last-five health checks. `HealthNote` is exported from this file at :13. */
  notes?: readonly HealthNote[];
}

export function renderRunCard(status: RunStatus, handlers: Partial<RunHandlers>, deps?: RunCardDeps): HTMLElement;
export function updateRunCard(card: HTMLElement, status: RunStatus, counters: RunCounters, deps?: RunCardDeps): void;
```

**The migration is sixteen call sites, not "four or five".** Two are product code (`builtin/tasks.ts:130` and `:132`, both inside `renderRun`, and both now reading `notes` out of Step 0b's history section) and fourteen are in `builtin/tasksViews.test.ts` (`:39`, `:44`, `:49`, `:51`, `:56`, `:61`, `:75`, `:82`, `:92`, `:96`, `:105`, `:114`, `:125`, `:130`, `:139`, `:154`). Every one of them passes `now` positionally and six of them pass `notes` positionally as well, so all sixteen move to `{ now, notes }`. Do that migration in Step 2, in the same commit; `npm run typecheck` lists them.

**Two of this task's new hooks collide with hooks SP4b already shipped, and both have to be renamed rather than reused:**

- `data-run-xp` is **taken**. `tasksViews.ts:126` puts it on the xp-per-hour detail line, and `updateRunCard` patches that line through it at `:159`. The counter row's session-xp figure therefore becomes `data-run-session-xp`, and the Step 1 test below reads that.
- `data-run-elapsed` is **taken** too, by the run card's existing `.run-elapsed num` span at `:118`, which is the same node this task restyles to 16px. Keep the hook and restyle the node; do not add a second one.

`data-run-logs` is genuinely new and free.

**Where the two new rows get their data, which nothing else in this plan says:**

- **The quest steps.** `Script.tasks` (`tasks/types.ts:236`) is the ordered task list, but it is deliberately out of reach: `TasksApiDeps` at `api.ts:87-91` narrows the library to manifests with the comment "the api never needs a script's `tasks`", and the executable `Script` lives in the Worker. The reachable source is the trace the recorder already keeps: each `task_enter` event names a task, and `run_done.tasksEntered: string[]` names them all for a finished run. So `steps` is derived from `task_enter`: everything before the last one is `done`, the last one is `current`, and **no live run can report `upcoming`**. The `upcoming` marker still ships in the CSS and on the styleguide, because the mock draws it and a replayed finished run has one, but the panel does not invent it. If a reconciliation finds SP4b has exposed the task list to the frame, use that instead and the row becomes the mock's three-state row.
- **The counters.** `RunStatus` carries neither, and neither is new work: `logs` is the active session's loot tracker count for the run's item and `sessionXp` is its xp tracker's total gained, both of which `main.ts:196-197` already hands to the XP and Loot plugins as `stage.activeXp` and `stage.activeLoot`. `TasksPluginDeps` gains `counters(): RunCounters` reading those two, and the panel calls `updateRunCard` from **the tick it already runs**; this task adds no timer.

The class set `layout/automation.css` owns: `.run-card`, `.run-title`, `.run-elapsed`, `.run-steps`, `.run-step`, `.run-step-mark`, `.run-step-label`, `.run-step-link`, `.run-counters`, `.run-actions`, `.task-row`, `.task-meta`, `.task-req`, `.history-row`, `.market-card`, `.automation-content`.

```ts
it('keeps every hook the suite and the e2e specs address', () => {
  const el = renderRunCard(runningStatus, handlers, deps);
  for (const hook of ['data-run-card', 'data-run-state', 'data-run-pause', 'data-run-stop', 'data-run-task']) {
    expect(el.querySelector(`[${hook}]`) ?? el.matches(`[${hook}]`), hook).toBeTruthy();
  }
});
it('draws the state badge at the large geometry, and the title and elapsed at the mock sizes', () => {
  const el = renderRunCard(runningStatus, handlers, deps);
  expect(el.querySelector('[data-run-state]')?.className).toBe('badge badge-lg badge-accent');
  expect(el.querySelector('.run-title')?.textContent).toBe('Chop and drop');
  // map-design S14 and ruling R16: README says 14px, the mock says 16px, and the mock wins. The
  // old assertion here was `className).toContain('run-elapsed')`, a class containing itself,
  // which passes for any element that has the class and pins neither the size nor the font.
  const css = readFileSync(new URL('../../styles/layout/automation.css', import.meta.url), 'utf8');
  expect(baseRule(css, '.run-elapsed')).toContain('font-size: 16px');
  expect(baseRule(css, '.run-elapsed')).toContain('font-family: var(--font-num)');
  expect(baseRule(css, '.run-title')).toContain('font-size: var(--fs-title)');
});
it('draws the quest steps as done, current and upcoming', () => {
  // `upcoming` is only reachable for a replayed finished run; see the Interfaces block. The
  // renderer takes whatever the caller derived and does not invent a step.
  const el = renderRunCard(runningStatus, handlers, { ...deps, steps: [{ label: 'Walk', state: 'done' }, { label: 'Chop tree', state: 'current' }, { label: 'Drop', state: 'upcoming' }] });
  expect([...el.querySelectorAll('.run-step')].map(s => s.getAttribute('data-step'))).toEqual(['done', 'current', 'upcoming']);
  expect(el.querySelector('.run-step[data-step="done"] .run-step-mark')?.textContent).toBe('✓');
});
it('shows Pause while running and Resume while paused or stuck, and Trace and Stop always', () => {
  const labels = (s: RunStatus) => [...renderRunCard(s, handlers, deps).querySelectorAll('.run-actions .btn')].map(b => b.textContent);
  expect(labels(runningStatus)).toEqual(['Pause', 'Trace', 'Stop']);
  expect(labels(pausedStatus)).toEqual(['Resume', 'Trace', 'Stop']);
  expect(labels(stuckStatus)).toEqual(['Resume', 'Trace', 'Stop']);
});
it('patches counters in place rather than rebuilding, so focus survives a tick', () => {
  const el = renderRunCard(runningStatus, handlers, deps);
  const node = el.querySelector('[data-run-logs]')!;
  updateRunCard(el, { ...runningStatus }, { logs: 42, sessionXp: 4205 }, deps);
  expect(el.querySelector('[data-run-logs]')).toBe(node);
  expect(node.textContent).toBe('42');
  // `data-run-session-xp`, not `data-run-xp`: SP4b Task 10 already owns the latter for the
  // xp-per-hour detail line at tasksViews.ts:126, and reusing it makes updateRunCard patch the
  // rate line with a total. See this task's Interfaces block.
  expect(el.querySelector('[data-run-session-xp]')?.textContent).toBe('4,205');
});
it('leaves SP4b\'s detail lines and health list where it found them', () => {
  const el = renderRunCard({ ...runningStatus, target: { via: 'scene', kind: 'tree', name: 'Oak', distance: 12 }, xpPerHour: { Woodcutting: 12_000 } }, handlers, { ...deps, notes: NOTES.slice(0, 2) });
  // Mutation target: a rewrite that folds these three into the new counter row fails here, which
  // is the whole reason the case exists.

  expect(el.querySelector('[data-run-xp]')?.textContent).toContain('12,000 Woodcutting xp/h');
  expect(el.querySelectorAll('[data-run-health-item]')).toHaveLength(2);
});
```

`layout/automation.css`:

```css
.run-card { display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 6px; }
/* map-design 3.9: `font-size:14.5px; font-weight:650; color:#e8e8e6`. Today's tasks.css has no
   title rule at all, so the script name inherits 12px/normal. */
.run-title { font-size: var(--fs-title); font-weight: 650; color: var(--text-strong); }
.run-elapsed { font-family: var(--font-num); font-weight: 700; font-size: 16px; font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; color: var(--accent-bright); }
.run-steps { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 5px; margin-top: 1px; }
.run-step { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 5px; }
.run-step-mark { display: grid; place-items: center; width: 14px; height: 14px; border-radius: 50%; font-size: 9px; }
.run-step[data-step="done"] .run-step-mark { background: rgba(67,160,71,.2); color: var(--ok-pop); }
.run-step[data-step="current"] .run-step-mark { background: rgba(255,152,31,.25); color: var(--accent); font-size: 8px; animation: stepPulse 1.8s ease-in-out infinite; }
.run-step[data-step="upcoming"] .run-step-mark { border: 1px solid #3a3a38; color: var(--text-faint); font-size: 8px; }
.run-step-label { font-size: var(--fs-tiny); color: var(--text-muted); }
.run-step[data-step="current"] .run-step-label { color: var(--accent); font-weight: 600; }
.run-step-link { width: 12px; height: 1px; background: #3a3a38; }
.run-counters { display: flex; gap: 8px; margin-top: 3px; padding: 6px 0 2px; border-top: 1px solid var(--hairline-soft); font-size: var(--fs-tiny); color: var(--text-muted); }
.run-counters b { font-family: var(--font-num); font-weight: 700; font-size: var(--fs-base); color: var(--text-strong); }
.run-counters b[data-run-session-xp] { color: var(--ok-pop); }
.run-actions { display: flex; gap: 6px; }
.run-actions .btn { flex: 1; }
```

`#3a3a38` is `--control-hover`; use the token.

- [ ] **Step 3: Restyle the script rows, the snippet and the history**

`renderScriptRow` becomes a `card({ class: 'task-row' })` with `card-lift`: a title row (name plus a `badge badge-sm` for the source, `SOURCE_TONE` at `tasksViews.ts:163` unchanged), an 11px description, a 10px `.task-meta` line, the amber requirement line **without** the `⚠` (ruling R19), and a `Run` `btn btn-primary btn-sm btn-in-card` plus `Fork` or `Edit` as `btn btn-sm`. `.btn-in-card` is what gives it the mock's `.25` resting glow and `.35` hover; without it the card button takes the co-pilot bar's `.28`/`.45`.

**SP4b Task 9's per-script toggle (`tasksViews.ts:200-206`) survives intact, and three things about its markup are load-bearing.** It is a `<label class="field field-inline task-row-toggle switch">` holding an `<input type="checkbox" data-task-enabled="<id>" aria-label="Enable <name>">` and a `<span class="field-label">` reading `On` or `Off`. The `switch` class is on the **label** and not on the input, because `form.css` draws the pill as `.switch input[type="checkbox"]` and on the input itself the rules match nothing and the row renders a bare browser checkbox. The `aria-label` on the box is not decoration either: the only text in the label is the state, so ten rows would otherwise announce ten controls all called "On". Keep all three, keep the toggle before the Run button, and keep Run's `disabled: off || !ok` and its `title` of `'This script is turned off'` (`:207-211`). Restyle only.

The Run snippet becomes `sectionLabel('Run snippet')` plus a `textarea code` at `rows="2"`.

`renderHistoryRow` becomes a `.history-row`: a 7px `dot` (`ok` for done, `error` for failed, idle for stopped, from `HISTORY_TONE` at `tasksViews.ts:259`), a name plus a **non-mono** meta line (note S17), and the existing button restyled to `btn btn-outline btn-xs`. **Its label is `Open report`, not `Trace`.** SP4b Task 11 changed it at `tasksViews.ts:297`, because what it opens is the report with the raw trace one click further in, and Task 16 below points it at the trace window without touching the label. `data-trace-open` stays as the hook; SP4b kept that name deliberately so `tasks.pw` keeps working.

**Three groups of rules SP4b added to the run card survive this task and are not folded into the new ones.** `renderRunCard` at HEAD emits, in order: the `[data-run-line]` status line (`:122`), the `[data-run-target]` detail line (`:125`), the `[data-run-xp]` xp-rate line (`:126`) and the `[data-run-health]` notes list (`:127`). The mock's quest-step row goes **above** the status line and the counter row goes **below** the notes list; nothing between them moves, and `updateRunCard`'s four `setLine` and `fillNotes` patches at `:156-160` keep working unchanged. Their CSS (`.run-detail-line`, `.run-health`, `.run-health-item` and its `::before`, moved to `layout/automation.css` by Task 2) is restyled to the v2 tokens in Step 2 and not deleted.
