### Task 16: The trace pop-out window (G8.2)

The trace becomes a window over the stage bottom right, not a block inside the Automation panel. It gets a stage-level host mirroring `#bank-host`, real window chrome, a free-text filter in place of the kind select, and a footer count.

Design source: `map-design.md` section 3.5 (the frame, the header, all eight seeded rows with their rails, the footer) and `map-contracts.md` section 2 in full.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/frame/traceWindow.ts`, `web/src/frame/traceWindow.test.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/partials/frame.html:16` (add `#trace-host`), `web/src/main.ts:216` (build the window, beside `createBankWindow(byId('bank-host'), ...)`), `web/src/plugins/builtin/traceView.ts` (157 lines: the row format, the filter, the copy label), `builtin/traceView.test.ts` (133 lines), `builtin/tasksHistory.ts` (Task 10 Step 0b's home for `openReportFor`), `builtin/runReportView.ts:135-145` (`traceToggle`, the report's inline mount), `web/src/styles/layout/trace.css`
- Test: `web/src/plugins/builtin/tasks.market.test.ts` (Task 10 Step 0 moved the report-and-trace cases there from `tasks.test.ts:203-238`). Grep for `data-trace-row`, not the line.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `renderTraceView`, `appendTraceEvent`, `describeEvent`, `UNTRUSTED_HEADER` from `builtin/traceView.ts`; `TraceEvent` from `web/src/tasks/types.ts`; the `.window` chrome from Task 7.
- Produces:

```ts
// web/src/frame/traceWindow.ts
export interface TraceWindowDeps {
  api(): TasksApi;
  copy(text: string): void;
  /**
   * Fired once whenever the window goes away by any route: the x, Escape, `close()` or
   * `dispose()`. The Automation panel consumes it to un-press its Trace button, and the co-pilot
   * bar to un-press its `Open trace`. Two tests in Step 2 assert on it, so it is part of the
   * interface and not an afterthought.
   */
  onClose?(): void;
  now?(): number;
}
export interface TraceWindow {
  open(runId: string, scriptName: string | null): Promise<void>;
  close(): void;
  isOpen(): boolean;
  /** Live append while the window shows the run the event belongs to. */
  push(e: TraceEvent): void;
  dispose(): void;
}
export function createTraceWindow(host: HTMLElement, deps: TraceWindowDeps): TraceWindow;
```

  - `web/src/plugins/builtin/runReportView.ts`: `RunReportOpts` gains `openTrace(runId: string, scriptName: string | null): void`, and `traceToggle` (`:135-145`) loses its `host` and its `aria-expanded`, because a button that opens a window is not a disclosure. Step 5 says why.

- [ ] **Step 1: G8 check 2, and its expected evidence**

Confirm before building. `map-contracts.md` section 2.1 has already run it:

```bash
grep -n "data-kind\|data-line" web/src/plugins/builtin/traceView.ts
grep -c "kind: '" web/src/tasks/types.ts    # 24, NOT 18: it also counts
                                            # TargetEvent and the other kind-carrying shapes
grep -rn "openTraceFor\|openReportFor" web/src/plugins/builtin/
```

Expected, all four re-confirmed at HEAD on 2026-09-07:
- Every `TraceEvent` is a discriminated union on `kind` with **18 variants** (`types.ts:255-273`; count the arms, not the grep hits, because the grep returns 24 where other shapes in the file carry a `kind` too), and `traceView.ts:63` stamps `data-kind="${e.kind}"`. **The 2px kind rails are pure CSS; no data change is needed.**
- `describeEvent` (`traceView.ts:21-42`) is an exhaustive switch over all eighteen, so a new kind is a compile error rather than a blank row. Keep it that way.
- The trace lives inside the Worker (`agent/worker.ts:208`), reaches the frame as `trace` messages through `WorkerHost.onTrace` then `RunRecorder` then `TasksApi.onEvent` then the router, and **the router drops background sessions** (`router.ts:174-175`). That is ruling R12's reason.
- **SP4b Task 11 has landed.** `openTraceFor` is gone; `openReportFor` is `tasks.ts:257` before Task 10 Step 0b moves it to `tasksHistory.ts`, and `renderTraceView` already has its second mount, at `runReportView.ts:140` inside `traceToggle`. If a grep still finds `openTraceFor`, the tree is older than this plan assumes and Step 5 below does not apply.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing window test**

```ts
it('renders the chrome the mock specifies: title, script, filter, copy, close, footer count', async () => {
  const win = createTraceWindow(host, deps);
  await win.open('r1', 'Chop and drop');
  const el = host.querySelector('.trace-window')!;
  expect(el.querySelector('.window-title')?.textContent).toBe('Trace');
  expect(el.querySelector('.window-sub')?.textContent).toBe('Chop and drop');
  expect(el.querySelector('[data-trace-filter]')?.getAttribute('placeholder')).toBe('Filter');
  expect(el.querySelector('[data-trace-copy]')?.textContent).toBe('Copy for Claude');
  expect(el.querySelector('[data-trace-close]')?.getAttribute('aria-label')).toBe('Close trace');
  expect(el.querySelector('.window-foot')?.textContent).toContain('8 events');
});

it('filters by substring against the whole line, not by kind', async () => {
  const win = createTraceWindow(host, deps);
  await win.open('r1', 'Chop and drop');
  const input = host.querySelector<HTMLInputElement>('[data-trace-filter]')!;
  input.value = 'Woodcutting';
  input.dispatchEvent(new Event('input'));
  const shown = [...host.querySelectorAll('[data-trace-row]')].filter(r => !(r as HTMLElement).hidden);
  expect(shown.length).toBe(2);
  // Mutation target: filtering on data-kind instead of data-line matches nothing here.
});

it('appends a live event only for the run it is showing', async () => {
  const win = createTraceWindow(host, deps);
  await win.open('r1', 'Chop and drop');
  // `types.ts:257` is `{ kind: 'xp'; skill: string; delta: number }`: skill is a NAME, there is
  // no runId on the variant, and `as TraceEvent` over a numeric skill is a non-overlapping
  // assertion that does not compile under strict TS. The run id comes from `open(runId)`.
  win.push({ seq: 9, at: 0, kind: 'xp', skill: 'Woodcutting', delta: 25 });
  expect(host.querySelectorAll('[data-trace-row]').length).toBe(9);
  expect(host.querySelector('.window-foot')?.textContent).toContain('9 events');
});

it('owns its element rather than reaching into the host first child (ruling R13)', async () => {
  const win = createTraceWindow(host, deps);
  host.appendChild(document.createElement('div'));    // something else got there first
  await win.open('r1', null);
  win.push(anXpEvent);
  expect(host.querySelectorAll('[data-trace-row]').length).toBe(9);
});

it('closes on Escape and on the close button, and fires onClose once each', async () => {
  const win = createTraceWindow(host, deps);
  await win.open('r1', 'Chop and drop');
  host.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>('[data-trace-close]')!.click();
  expect(host.querySelector('.trace-window')).toBeNull();
  expect(deps.onClose).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);

  await win.open('r1', 'Chop and drop');
  document.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: 'Escape' }));
  expect(host.querySelector('.trace-window')).toBeNull();
  expect(deps.onClose).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);
});

it('never takes Escape away from the panic key', async () => {
  // The Global Constraints say the Esc panic-key contract does not move, and Task 14's bar keeps
  // runBanner's document keydown that calls api.pause('player') while running. So the window's
  // handler follows the convention bank/view.ts:205 already established -- return early when
  // `event.defaultPrevented`, and never call preventDefault itself -- and one Escape does both.
  const win = createTraceWindow(host, deps);
  bar.update(runningStatus);
  await win.open('r1', 'Chop and drop');
  document.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: 'Escape' }));
  expect(host.querySelector('.trace-window')).toBeNull();
  expect(api.pause).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
  expect(api.pause).toHaveBeenCalledWith('player');
});

it('fires nothing after dispose: no append, no listener, no element', async () => {
  const win = createTraceWindow(host, deps);
  await win.open('r1', null);
  win.dispose();
  win.push(anXpEvent);
  expect(host.querySelector('.trace-window')).toBeNull();
  expect(deps.onClose).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);       // dispose closed it, once
  document.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: 'Escape' }));
  // Mutation target: a dispose() that clears the element but leaves the document listener bound
  // takes this to 2. Still 1 is the proof the listener is gone, not just gated.
  expect(deps.onClose).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Build the window and its host**

`frame.html`, beside `#bank-host`:

```html
        <div id="trace-host" class="trace-host"></div>
```

**The Escape rule, in one sentence: the panic key always wins.** The window's `keydown` handler is `if (event.key !== 'Escape' || event.defaultPrevented) return;` (the convention `bank/view.ts:205` already sets) and it **never calls `preventDefault` or `stopPropagation`**. One Escape over a live run therefore closes the window and pauses the run, which is correct: the panic key is the one interaction the sprint says must not move, and a window that swallowed it would disable it silently. `frame/stage.ts:195-206`'s iframe re-dispatch is untouched.

`main.ts`, beside `createBankWindow(byId('bank-host'), ...)`:

```ts
const traceWindow = createTraceWindow(byId('trace-host'), { api: () => tasksRouter.api, copy: writeClipboard });
// `main.ts:216` is where `createBankWindow(byId('bank-host'), ...)` already sits; put this beside it.
tasksRouter.api.onEvent(e => traceWindow.push(e));
tasksRouter.onActiveChanged(() => traceWindow.close());   // ruling R12
```

`createTraceWindow` composes `.window .window-docked .trace-window` (Task 7 owns the first two) with the mock's own size in `layout/trace.css`:

```css
/* Size only. The lane comes from `.window` (`var(--z-stage-window)`) and the placement from
   `.window-docked`, both from Task 7; writing either here would be the per-surface one-off the
   styles rule exists to stop. The element's class list is `window window-docked trace-window`. */
.trace-window { width: 430px; max-width: calc(100% - 32px); }
.trace-window .window-body { max-height: 250px; padding: 5px 0; font-family: var(--font-num); font-size: var(--fs-tiny); }
.trace-row { padding: 2.5px 10px; border-left: 2px solid transparent; color: var(--text); }
.trace-row[data-kind="task_enter"], .trace-row[data-kind="run_started"] { border-left-color: var(--accent); }
.trace-row[data-kind="run_done"] { border-left-color: var(--accent); color: var(--text-strong); }
.trace-row[data-kind="action"], .trace-row[data-kind="target"] { border-left-color: var(--info); }
.trace-row[data-kind="xp"], .trace-row[data-kind="item"] { border-left-color: var(--ok); }
.trace-row[data-kind="log"], .trace-row[data-kind="status"], .trace-row[data-kind="snapshot"] { color: var(--text-muted); }
.trace-row[data-kind="stuck"], .trace-row[data-kind="paused"], .trace-row[data-kind="health"] { border-left-color: var(--warn); color: var(--warn); }
.trace-row[data-kind="task_exit"], .trace-row[data-kind="resumed"], .trace-row[data-kind="recovery"], .trace-row[data-kind="human_input"] { border-left-color: var(--text-faint); }
.trace-row[data-kind="truncated"] { border-left-color: var(--error); color: var(--text-faint); }
```

The mock shows six of the eighteen kinds; the eleven it does not show are assigned above by family, and `truncated` gets the error rail because a dropped trace is a data loss the player should see.

- [ ] **Step 4: Fix the three copy and format drifts in `traceView.ts`**

Per `map-contracts.md` section 8.9, and the mock wins on all three:

1. `traceView.ts:151` reads `Copy to Claude`; the mock reads **`Copy for Claude`**, as does the Events footer and as `runReportView.ts:168` already does for the report's own copy button. The button moves into the window header, so the string moves with it, and afterwards the two say the same words.
2. `offset()` (`traceView.ts:45`) formats `+3.9s`; the mock formats `00:03.9` (mm:ss.d). Rewrite it and update `traceView.test.ts`.
3. `describeEvent` writes prose (`→ Chop tree`); the mock leads with the raw kind token (`task_enter Chop tree`). Prefix each line with `e.kind` and keep the prose after it.

`applyFilter` (`:82-86`) becomes a case-insensitive substring test against `data-line`, and the `<select>` (`:135`) and `addKindOption` (`:88-91`) are deleted; the kind rails make the select redundant and the mock has a text input.

**`data-line` already exists, which the plan had wrong.** `traceView.ts:63` stamps `data-trace-row`, `data-kind`, **`data-line`** and `data-at` in one call, and `data-line` carries exactly what this step needs: `${offset(at - t0)} ${describeEvent(e)}`, the whole rendered text of the row. SP4b Task 11 added it so the copy button could join the **visible** lines (`:147-150` filters on `!hidden && dataset.line`). So the filter has its attribute and this step adds no data; what it does is change what `describeEvent` puts in it, per drift 3 above, which changes `data-line` and the copy output together and is correct. Keep the existing `traceView.test.ts` coverage of the copy button (`:66-80`) and add a case asserting a row's `data-line` still equals its own `textContent` after the format change, so the two cannot drift apart.

**Three things `appendTraceEvent` does that the select's deletion breaks, and all three are in `traceView.ts:112-128`:** it early-returns unless it finds **both** `[data-trace-rows]` and `[data-trace-filter]` (`:113-115`), it reads `select.value` to decide the new row's `hidden` (`:124`), and it calls `addKindOption(select, e.kind)` (`:127`). With the select gone, the guard has to drop to `rows` alone or every live append silently does nothing; the `hidden` decision becomes the same case-insensitive substring test against the input's value; and the `addKindOption` call goes. `mergeTraceEvent` (`:106-110`) is untouched and must stay exported: Task 10 Step 0b's history section calls it so the report's trace survives a collapse and re-expand, and `tasks.test.ts:219-238` is the case that proves it.

**Three `traceView.test.ts` cases type the filter as an `HTMLSelectElement`** and drive it with `select.value = 'xp'` plus a `change` event: `:44-54`, `:66-80` and `:82-93`. All three fail to compile against an `<input>`. Rewrite each to `HTMLInputElement`, a substring value and an `input` event, keeping what each one is actually about (hiding rows, copying visible lines, appending live).

`UNTRUSTED_HEADER` (`traceView.ts:12`) stays exactly as it is, and so does `runReportView.ts:45`'s use of it.

- [ ] **Step 5: Point the report's trace toggle and the two Trace buttons at the window**

**The trace is one level deeper than this step was written against, and the change is smaller because of it.** There is no inline `traceHost` in the panel any more: SP4b Task 11 replaced it with `reportHost`, which mounts a whole `renderRunReport` (`tasks.ts:263-272`, moved to `tasksHistory.ts` by Task 10 Step 0b), and the raw trace sits behind that report's own `Show trace` toggle (`runReportView.ts:135-145`). So:

- The history row's `data-trace-open` keeps opening the **report**, not the window. Its label already says `Open report` and neither changes. The report stays in the panel; it is the thing a player came to the History section for.
- The report's `Show trace` toggle (`runReportView.ts:137`) is what moves: instead of mounting `renderTraceView` into `.report-trace`, it calls `traceWindow.open(summary.runId, summary.scriptName)`. Its label becomes `Open trace` and its `aria-expanded` goes, because a button that opens a window is not a disclosure. `RunReportOpts` gains `openTrace(runId: string, scriptName: string | null): void` and `traceToggle` loses its `host`; `renderRunReport`'s trailing `trace.host` child (`:171`) goes with it, and so does the `.report-trace:empty` rule at `tasks.css:65`.
- The run card's `Trace` button (Task 15 Step 1's `['Pause', 'Trace', 'Stop']`) and the co-pilot bar's stuck-state `Open trace` (Task 14) both call the same `traceWindow.open`.

**`renderTraceView` therefore has exactly one mount after this task, not two**, which is the opposite of what ruling R13 assumed while SP4b was in flight and does not weaken R13: the window still owns its own element reference, because `host.firstElementChild` is not a valid handle for a host that anything else may append to, and the Step 2 case that proves it stays.

The two cases asserting an inline `[data-trace-row]` list inside the panel (`tasks.test.ts:203-217` and `:219-238` at HEAD, moved to `tasks.market.test.ts` by Task 10 Step 0) are rewritten against the window: the first asserts that `[data-report-trace]` opens the window rather than filling `.report-trace`, and the second, which is about a live row surviving a collapse and re-expand, becomes a live row surviving a window close and re-open, still through `mergeTraceEvent`. Grep `data-trace-row` and `data-report-trace` rather than trusting either set of numbers.

The co-pilot bar's stuck-state `Open trace` button (Task 14) calls the same `open`.

### Task 17: The bank window and the bank panel (G8.3)

A 470px centred window on the warm `#252220` ground, with a left tab rail, an 8-column grid and the footer README never describes.

Design source: `map-design.md` section 3.6 (the whole window, including the footer README omits and the dashed add-tab button) and 3.13 (the panel), and `map-contracts.md` section 3.

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/src/bank/view.ts`, `bank/grid.ts`, `bank/tabsBar.ts`, `bank/bottomBar.ts`, `web/src/plugins/builtin/bank.ts`, `web/src/styles/layout/bank.css`, `web/styleguide.html`
- Test: `web/src/bank/view.test.ts` (357 lines, at the ceiling: **split first**), `bank/view.a11y.test.ts`, `bank/grid.test.ts`, `bank/tabsBar.test.ts`, `web/src/styles/bank.test.ts`, `web/e2e/bank-ui.pw.test.ts` (380 lines, at the ceiling: **split first**)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the `.window` chrome from Task 7, **as `.window .window-warm .window-centred`**; `badge` from `el.ts`; `meter` from `parts.ts`.
- Produces: no new module. `bank/view.ts` splits into `view.ts` (the window and its header) and `bottomBar.ts` (the existing file, taking the mode, quantity, search and caption row).

**The bank is the family's one modifier, and Task 7 already wrote it (decision D22).** Do not restyle the bank window's ground, header gradient or shadow here: `.window-warm` carries all three, and `.window-centred` carries the placement and the header's `8px 12px` padding. `layout/bank.css` keeps the width (470px), the tab rail, the grid, the footer and the slot chrome, and nothing else. The Script Studio (sprint entry 6) composes the unmodified default beside this, which is what makes the split worth having.

**One `z-index` moves in this task.** `bank.css:16` puts `.bank-host` at `calc(var(--z-dialog) - 1)`, which is 49 and above everything but a `<dialog>`. Task 1 Step 3's lanes replace it: `.bank-host` takes `var(--z-stage-window)`, `.bank-window` writes no `z-index` of its own because `.window` already carries the lane, and `bank.test.ts:87`'s `expect(css).toContain('z-index: calc(var(--z-dialog) - 1)')` becomes `'z-index: var(--z-stage-window)'`. Without this the trace window from Task 16 renders underneath the bank, which is what the mock's own 30-over-25 says must not happen.

- [ ] **Step 1: G8 check 3, and its expected evidence**


### Task 18: The online timer (G3)

The active character tab shows a live `1:29:07` in place of the word `online`. The stamp is new, the render is a per-second patch in place, and the tab's full `innerHTML` rebuild is the wrong shape for a 1 Hz tick.

Contract source: `map-contracts.md` section 5 in full, and ruling R8.

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/src/sessions/types.ts:13-22` (`CharacterSession`), `sessions/manager.ts:98-109,164-200` (the stamp goes beside `lastStateAt` at `:107` and the initial `startedAt` at `:170`), `web/src/frame/characterTabs.ts`, `web/src/frame/stage.ts:249-256` (`renderTabs`; unchanged by SP4b)
- Test: `web/src/sessions/manager.test.ts` (396 lines: **split first**), `web/src/frame/characterTabs.test.ts:30-40,76,80,134,139,141`, `web/e2e/tabs.pw.test.ts:30`

`characterTabs.test.ts:36-39` is a **strict `toEqual`** over two whole slot objects (`{ kind, index, characterId, label, status, active, disabled, tooltip }`). Adding `clock` and `onlineSince` to the `character` arm of `TabSlot` gives both objects two more keys and that assertion fails; the claim that "computeSlots's existing pure tests are unaffected" is only true of the `toMatchObject` cases beside it. Convert those two to `toMatchObject`, and give the file's `input()` helper an `onlineSince: {}` default, because `TabsInput` gains that field as required.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `deriveSessionState`, `displayStatus` from `sessions/types.ts`.
- Produces:
  - `CharacterSession` gains `onlineSince: number | null`.
  - `web/src/frame/characterTabs.ts` exports `export function onlineFor(seconds: number): string` and `createCharacterTabs(...)` returns `{ render(input), tick(now: number) }`.
  - `TabsInput` gains `onlineSince: Record<string, number | null>`.

```ts
  expect(session.onlineSince).toBe(5_000);
  clock = 9_000; m.refreshAll();                    // still online: the stamp does NOT move
  expect(session.onlineSince).toBe(5_000);
});

it('clears onlineSince on every transition out of online', () => {

    const { m, session } = onlineSession();
    goOffline(m, leave);
    expect(session.onlineSince, leave).toBeNull();
  }
});

it('re-stamps on a reconnect rather than resuming the old clock', () => {

  goOffline(m, 'disconnect');
  clock = 20_000; setLoggedIn(true); m.refreshAll();
  expect(session.onlineSince).toBe(20_000);
});

it('does not confuse startedAt with onlineSince', () => {
  // startedAt has meant "the iframe object was created", in state booting, since SP7. A frame
  // parked on the title screen for two hours would read 2:00:00 if the tab used it (ruling R8).
  const { session } = onlineSession();
  expect(session.startedAt).toBeLessThan(session.onlineSince!);
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the stamp**

`sessions/types.ts`, in `CharacterSession`:

```ts
  /**
   * When this character last came ONLINE, or null while it is not. Distinct from `startedAt`,
   * which is when the iframe object was created, in state `booting`: the tab's clock is an
   * online timer, so a frame parked on the title screen must read nothing, not two hours.
   * Frame-local and never persisted: a reload restarting the clock is correct, the session did
   * restart.
   */
  onlineSince: number | null;
```

`sessions/manager.ts`, inside `refresh(entry)` beside `entry.session.lastStateAt = now()`:

```ts
  // Placed immediately AFTER `entry.session.lastStateAt = now()`. No `entry.session.state !==
  // 'online'` guard: `refresh` returns early at manager.ts:106 when the state has not moved, and
  // then assigns `entry.session.state = next` at :107, so by this point the two are equal and
  // such a guard is always false. The early return is the proof that a transition happened.
  entry.session.onlineSince = next === 'online' ? now() : null;
```

That one site is the single authority. The `login`, `logout` and `disconnect` hooks (`manager.ts:139-141`) and the 1 s poll (`:117`) all call `refresh`, so it covers every path, including a client that was already in game when the frame attached, which `login()` at `:265` explicitly handles. Initialise `onlineSince: null` in the object literal at `manager.ts:168-171`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the failing tab test**

```ts
import { onlineFor } from './characterTabs';

describe('onlineFor', () => {
  it('drops the hour segment below an hour and pads the rest', () => {
    expect(onlineFor(0)).toBe('0:00');
    expect(onlineFor(67)).toBe('1:07');
    expect(onlineFor(3599)).toBe('59:59');
  });
  it('shows h:mm:ss from an hour up, matching the mock', () => {
    // .dc.html renderVals(): osec 5347 renders 1:29:07.
    expect(onlineFor(5347)).toBe('1:29:07');
    expect(onlineFor(3600)).toBe('1:00:00');
  });
});

describe('the tab clock', () => {
  it('renders the clock for an online character and the word for every other state', () => {
    const tabs = createCharacterTabs(root, deps);
    // 5,347 SECONDS is 1:29:07. `tick` takes MILLISECONDS, so the gap has to be 5,348,000ms,
    // not 5,347: `tick(6_347)` with `onlineSince: 1_000` is five seconds and renders `0:05`.
    tabs.render({ ...input, states: { c1: 'online', c2: 'offline' }, onlineSince: { c1: 1_000, c2: null } });
    tabs.tick(5_348_000);
    expect(status('c1')?.textContent).toBe('1:29:07');
    expect(status('c1')?.className).toContain('num');
    expect(status('c2')?.textContent).toBe('offline');
    expect(status('c2')?.className).not.toContain('num');
  });

  it('patches the clock in place, keeping focus and the roving tabindex', () => {
    const tabs = createCharacterTabs(root, deps);
    tabs.render({ ...input, states: { c1: 'online' }, onlineSince: { c1: 0 } });
    const node = status('c1')!;
    const tab = root.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[data-char-tab="c1"]')!;
    tab.focus();
    tabs.tick(61_000);
    // Mutation target: calling render() from tick() replaces the node and blurs the tab.
    expect(status('c1')).toBe(node);
    expect(node.textContent).toBe('1:01');
    expect(document.activeElement).toBe(tab);
    expect(tab.tabIndex).toBe(0);
  });

  it("keeps the tooltip's word, so 'alpha · online' still reads as a state", () => {
    const tabs = createCharacterTabs(root, deps);
    tabs.render({ ...input, states: { c1: 'online' }, onlineSince: { c1: 0 } });
    expect(root.querySelector('[data-char-tab="c1"]')?.getAttribute('title')).toBe('alpha · online');
  });

  it('tick is a no-op for a tab with no stamp, and never throws', () => {
    const tabs = createCharacterTabs(root, deps);
    tabs.render({ ...input, states: { c1: 'connecting' }, onlineSince: { c1: null } });
    expect(() => tabs.tick(9_999)).not.toThrow();
    expect(status('c1')?.textContent).toBe('connecting');
  });
});
```

The tooltip case matters: `characterTabs.test.ts:37` asserts `'alpha · online'` today, and the tooltip is a *state*, not a clock. Keeping the word there costs nothing and preserves the assertion.

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement the clock**

```ts
/** The mock's own format, from renderVals(): 5347 renders `1:29:07`, 67 renders `1:07`. */
export function onlineFor(seconds: number): string {
  const h = Math.floor(seconds / 3600);
  const m = Math.floor((seconds % 3600) / 60);
  const s = String(seconds % 60).padStart(2, '0');
  return h > 0 ? `${h}:${String(m).padStart(2, '0')}:${s}` : `${m}:${s}`;
}
```

`computeSlots` fills the `clock` field Task 8 added to the `character` slot arm:

```ts
      const since = input.onlineSince[character.id] ?? null;
      slots.push({
        kind: 'character', index, characterId: character.id, label: character.gameName,
        status, active: character.id === input.activeId, disabled: false,
        tooltip: `${character.gameName} · ${status}`,
        // A BOOLEAN, not an empty string: `slot.clock ? ' num' : ''` in slotHtml is a truthiness
        // test, and `''` is falsy, so a string marker would never add the `num` class and the
        // case below asserting it would fail.
        clock: status === 'online' && since !== null,
        onlineSince: since
      });
```

`clock` says "this tab renders a clock"; `render()` writes `slot.status` into the span and stamps `data-online-since`, then calls `tick(deps.now())` as its last statement so an online tab never paints the word `online` for a frame before the clock replaces it. `computeSlots` stays pure and free of the clock itself, which is what keeps its own tests cheap.

`slotHtml` emits `data-online-since` on the same span as `data-tab-status`, or `tick`'s query below matches nothing:

```ts
    tick(now: number): void {
      for (const el of root.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('[data-tab-status][data-online-since]')) {
        const since = Number(el.dataset.onlineSince);
        el.textContent = onlineFor(Math.max(0, Math.floor((now - since) / 1000)));
      }
    }
```

Nothing else in the tab is touched, so focus and the roving tabindex survive, which is what the `runBanner` learned the hard way and what SP4b's `updateRunCard` does for the same reason.

- [ ] **Step 6: Drive it**

`frame/stage.ts` already renders the tabs from `sessions.states()` at `:252`; add `onlineSince` from the same walk. Arm one interval in the stage, at 1000ms, calling `tabs.tick(Date.now())`, and clear it in the stage's dispose. One interval for the whole strip, not one per tab.

- [ ] **Step 7: Update `tabs.pw.test.ts:30`**

It polls `[data-tab-status]` textContent for `'online'`. The active tab now shows a clock, so poll for `/^\d+:\d{2}(:\d{2})?$/` instead, and keep a separate assertion that an offline tab still reads `offline`.

Design source: `map-design.md` sections 3.8 (the pinned card and the pin logic), 3.10 (the two-card XP layout README never describes) and 3.11 (the loot empty state).

```ts
it('the unpin button is labelled and calls back exactly once', () => {
  const pinned = createPinnedTracker(host, { rows: () => [wcRow], onUnpin });
  pinned.setPinned(true);
  const btn = host.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>('[data-unpin]')!;
  expect(btn.getAttribute('aria-label')).toBe('Unpin');
  // map-design 3.8 draws it at 18x18 and 12px, which is `.btn-icon-xs`, not the window close.
  expect(btn.className).toBe('btn btn-icon btn-icon-xs');
  btn.click();
  expect(onUnpin).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
});
it('hides itself when unpinned and renders nothing after dispose', () => {
  const pinned = createPinnedTracker(host, { rows: () => [wcRow], onUnpin });
  pinned.setPinned(true);
  pinned.setPinned(false);
  expect(host.classList.contains('hidden')).toBe(true);
  expect(host.children.length).toBe(0);
  pinned.dispose();
  pinned.render();
  expect(host.children.length).toBe(0);
});
```

### Task 21: The regression fixtures and the completeness rule (library parts 2 and 3)

The last two halves of "living rather than documented": a screenshot baseline per component family, and a test that says a component which is not on the styleguide does not exist.

The five `components/**/*.card.html` files cannot render as shipped: each references `../../_ds_bundle.js`, which is not in the vendored bundle, plus React and Babel from unpkg (`map-design.md` note S26). Ruling R26 therefore uses them as the **variant checklist** and takes the baselines from the styleguide, which is our rendering of the same system and the only one that exists.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/styleguide.families.test.ts`, `web/e2e/styleguide.pw.test.ts`, `web/e2e/styleguide.pw.test.ts-snapshots/` (five committed PNGs)
- Modify: `web/styleguide.html` (section ids and `data-sg-family` attributes), `web/src/styles/families.ts` (the family-to-section map)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `FAMILIES` from `web/src/styles/families.ts`.
- Produces:
  - `families.ts` gains `export const STYLEGUIDE_SECTIONS: Record<string, string>` mapping a family stylesheet to its styleguide section id.
  - `families.ts` gains `export const CARD_FAMILIES = ['core', 'data', 'forms', 'navigation', 'overlay'] as const;`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the completeness rule**

`web/src/styleguide.families.test.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/styleguide.families.test.ts -- library part 2's teeth: a component that is not on the
// styleguide does not exist, and adding a family without its styleguide entry is an incomplete
// change (companion spec section 5).
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { CARD_FAMILIES, FAMILIES, STYLEGUIDE_SECTIONS } from './styles/families';

const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
const guide = readFileSync(join(here, '../styleguide.html'), 'utf8');
const css = (name: string): string => readFileSync(join(here, 'styles', name), 'utf8');
// Comments FIRST. Every family stylesheet this plan writes opens with a path comment naming the
// component file it implements ("components/core/Button.{jsx,d.ts,prompt.md}"), and a raw scan
// turns `.jsx`, `.ts`, `.md` and `.css` into phantom classes that are in no demo, so `missing`
// could never be empty and the rule could never go green. `rulesOf` in styles.test.ts already
// strips them; this one has to as well.
const strip = (text: string): string => text.replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, '');
const classesIn = (text: string): Set<string> =>
  new Set([...strip(text).matchAll(/\.([a-zA-Z][\w-]*)/g)].map(m => m[1]));

describe('the styleguide covers the library', () => {
  it('has a section for every family stylesheet', () => {
    for (const name of Object.keys(FAMILIES)) {
      const id = STYLEGUIDE_SECTIONS[name];
      expect(id, `${name} has no styleguide section`).toBeDefined();
      expect(guide).toContain(`id="${id}"`);
    }
  });

  it('demonstrates every class each family defines', () => {
    // Collected from `class="..."` attributes only, and with the `.sg-code` strips removed
    // first. Scanning the page as text would let a class satisfy the rule by being NAMED in the
    // code strip at the bottom of its section rather than rendered: `.btn-block`, `.alert-title`
    // and `.badge-quiet` are all named there, so the mutation target this test claims -- adding
    // `.btn-huge` to button.css without a demo -- would only fail if the author also forgot to
    // add it to the strip. That is the whole of library part 2's teeth.
    const used = new Set(
      [...guide.replace(/<div class="sg-code">[\s\S]*?<\/div>/g, '').matchAll(/class="([^"]*)"/g)]
        .flatMap(m => m[1].split(/\s+/))
        .filter(c => c.length > 0 && !c.startsWith('sg-'))
    );
    const missing: string[] = [];
    for (const name of Object.keys(FAMILIES)) {
      for (const cls of classesIn(css(name))) {
        // Pseudo-state variants are exercised by their base class.
        if (!used.has(cls)) missing.push(`${name}: .${cls}`);
      }
    }
    // Mutation target: adding `.btn-huge` to button.css without a demo must fail here.
    expect(missing).toEqual([]);
  });

  it('exercises every component the five bundle demo cards do', () => {
    // The allowed set is the bundle's OWN component list: one `.d.ts` per component, across all
    // five families, which is nineteen names. It has to be the union and not the family's own
    // directory, because data.card.html renders <Badge>, <Button> and <SectionLabel>, which live
    // in core/. Everything else a card names is a local helper defined inside it -- every card
    // has a `function Demo()` and core.card.html has `const Row = ({children}) => ...` -- and
    // requiring `data-sg-component="Demo"` would put junk in the styleguide to satisfy a test.
    const components = new Set(CARD_FAMILIES.flatMap(f =>
      readdirSync(join(here, `../../docs/design/idlescape-shell-v2/components/${f}`))
        .filter(n => n.endsWith('.d.ts'))
        .map(n => basename(n, '.d.ts'))
    ));
    expect(components.size).toBe(19);
    for (const family of CARD_FAMILIES) {
      const card = readFileSync(join(here, `../../docs/design/idlescape-shell-v2/components/${family}/${family}.card.html`), 'utf8');
      // The cards are React; what they name is a component, not a class. The map from a component
      // name to our class is in the README's "Component library, statically" table.
      for (const component of [...card.matchAll(/<([A-Z]\w+)/g)].map(m => m[1])) {
        if (!components.has(component)) continue;
        expect(guide, `${family}.card.html exercises <${component}> and the styleguide does not`)
          .toContain(`data-sg-component="${component}"`);
      }
    }
  });

  it('keeps the styleguide under its own exempted ceiling', () => {
    // The Global Constraints exempt this one file from the 400-line rule and cap it at 1,200
    // instead; crossing that is the signal to split it into one partial per family.
    expect(guide.split('\n').length).toBeLessThan(1_200);
  });
});
```

Add `import { readdirSync } from 'node:fs';` and `basename` to the `node:path` import.

The third case is why each styleguide demo carries `data-sg-component="Button"` and so on: the demo cards name components, our page has classes, and this attribute is the join. Add it to one representative demo per component; nineteen attributes across the whole page.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the screenshot spec**

`web/e2e/styleguide.pw.test.ts`. It runs against `/styleguide.html` from the same `build:e2e` output the rest of the suite uses; nothing new is served.

```ts
import { expect, test } from '@playwright/test';
import { CARD_FAMILIES } from '../src/styles/families';

// One family spans several sections: core.card.html exercises Button, Badge, Tag, StatusDot,
// Alert, SectionLabel and Icon, and this plan deliberately splits those across four sections.
// Mapping `core` to `#buttons` alone would let a token change that moved a badge height or an
// alert rail pass the baseline it was written to catch. One named snapshot per section also
// keeps each PNG small enough to review by eye at Step 3.
const SECTIONS: Record<(typeof CARD_FAMILIES)[number], readonly string[]> = {
  core: ['#buttons', '#badges', '#alerts', '#icons'],
  data: ['#data'], forms: ['#forms'], navigation: ['#navigation'], overlay: ['#overlay']
};

test.describe('component library baselines', () => {
  for (const family of CARD_FAMILIES) {
    for (const id of SECTIONS[family]) {
      test(`${family} family matches its baseline at ${id}`, async ({ page }) => {
        await page.goto('/styleguide.html');
        // Fonts and the two infinite animations are the only sources of flake here. `.then(() =>
        // undefined)` matters: document.fonts.ready resolves with a FontFaceSet, and Playwright
        // serialises what an evaluate resolves to, so returning it directly throws -- in the one
        // guard written to remove flake.
        await page.evaluate(() => document.fonts.ready.then(() => undefined));
        await page.addStyleTag({ content: '*, *::before, *::after { animation: none !important; transition: none !important; }' });
        await expect(page.locator(id)).toHaveScreenshot(`${family}-${id.slice(1)}.png`, { maxDiffPixelRatio: 0.01 });
      });
    }
  }
});
```

Disabling animation is not cosmetic: `shimmer`, `barflow`, `pulse`, `bob` and `stepPulse` are all `infinite`, and a baseline taken mid-shimmer never matches twice. A token change that alters a button's height still fails the test, which is the point.

- [ ] **Step 3: Generate and commit the eight baselines**

With the stack up:

```bash
cd web && npm run build:e2e && npx playwright test styleguide --update-snapshots
```

Inspect all eight PNGs by eye against the mock before committing them. A baseline committed from a wrong render pins the wrong thing forever, and nothing downstream will ever question it.

```ts
  'button.css': 'buttons', 'badge.css': 'badges', 'card.css': 'data', 'meter.css': 'data',
  'form.css': 'forms', 'alert.css': 'alerts', 'overlay.css': 'overlay', 'strip.css': 'navigation',
  'tab.css': 'navigation', 'icon.css': 'icons'
};
```

## Notes for the executor

### Dependency order

Strictly sequential where a task consumes a name the previous one produces. The chain is:

```
1 tokens
 -> 2 CSS split -> 3 .p-* retirement
      -> 4 core -> 5 forms -> 6 data -> 7 overlay -> 8 navigation + frame chrome
           -> 9 icons -> 10 migrations
                -> 11 event bus -> 12 Events panel
                                -> 13 canvas overlays
                -> 14 co-pilot bar -> 15 Automation surfaces
                                   -> 16 trace window
                -> 17 bank
                -> 18 online timer
                -> 19 XP, pin, empty states
                -> 20 the five panels
                     -> 21 fixtures and the completeness rule
```

Task 21 must be **last**: its completeness rule fails while any family lacks a styleguide section, and its baselines pin whatever the styleguide renders at that moment.

### What can run in parallel

Tasks 4 to 7 are four independent family stylesheets plus four independent builder groups. They all append to `web/src/ui/parts.ts` and `web/styleguide.html`, so parallel worktrees will conflict in exactly two files and nowhere else. That is a cheap merge if each task appends at the end of both files and never reorders. Running 4 and 5 in parallel, then 6 and 7 in parallel, is the useful shape.

Tasks 13, 17, 18, 19 and 20 are genuinely independent of one another once 10 has landed: different panels, different stylesheets, no shared module. Task 13 needs 11 (the bus) and Task 15 needs 14; everything else in that group is free. **Tasks 17, 18 and 20 are the best parallel batch in the plan** - three worktrees, three reviewers, no shared file.

Tasks 11, 14 and 16 all touch `web/src/main.ts` and should not be parallel with each other.

### The worktree junction hazard

From `2026-09-06-sprint-handoff.md` section 4, and it has cost real time twice:

- Worktrees are created by hand under `.claude/worktrees/`, branched **from trunk, not from a sibling task's branch**. Twice in SP8b an implementer patched a stale copy of an already-merged file because its worktree was cut from another task's branch.
- `node_modules` is junctioned into each worktree. **`git worktree remove` follows the junction and empties the main tree's installs** - plain `remove`, not just `--force`.
- Unlink from PowerShell first, verify nothing remains, then remove one worktree at a time.
- Recovery is `npm ci` in `web/` **and `firebase/`** plus `bun install` in `server/` and `client/`. Do not forget `firebase/`: nothing exercises it until `verify.ps1` step 5, so a partial recovery looks complete for hours.
- Copy `web/.env.local` and `server/.env` into every new worktree.

### The SP4b reconciliation pass

**Done, on 2026-09-07, at `9608f2b`.** SP4b landed all fifteen of its tasks, its ledger is promoted to `docs/superpowers/ledgers/2026-09-06-sp4b-bot-expansion.md`, and this plan has been re-read against the tree file by file. The `Reconcile against HEAD before dispatch` lines are gone from the twelve places that carried them, and what moved is recorded in the last section of this file, "Reconciled against HEAD on 2026-09-07". Read that section before dispatching any task; it is the difference between what this plan says and what the tree holds.

**Task 14 was the one that could silently destroy work, and it no longer can.** It deletes `runBanner.ts`, which grew from about 160 lines to 302 under SP4b Task 10. All nine behaviours that growth added are now enumerated in a table inside Task 14 itself, each with the line it lives on today, the node it moves to on the bar, and the `runBanner.test.ts` case that proves it. Task 15 had the same shape of risk in the run card, and its `notes` parameter and two colliding data hooks are named in its Interfaces block for the same reason.

### Rulings that need an owner only if they turn out wrong

Three rulings are reversible cheaply and two are not:

- **R1 (em dashes) is one file.** `web/src/ui/copy.ts` holds all twelve strings, eight constants and four templates, and `copy.test.ts` enumerates them. If the owner wants hyphens, it is a twelve-line edit plus a baseline refresh.
- **R26 (the `.card.html` fixtures)** is a test-shape decision. If a way to render the demo cards appears, the baselines move without touching any product code.
- **R19 (the `⚠`)** is one glyph.
- **R2 (the token freeze)** is not cheap to reverse: the alternative is one 3,000-line commit. Decide it before Task 1, not after.
- **R22 (one run-state vocabulary)** is not cheap to reverse either: `presentRun` becomes load-bearing for the bar, the run card and the strip dot from Task 14 onward.

### Should this be two plans?

It could reasonably split at the Task 10 / Task 11 boundary: **library and migrations** (Tasks 1 to 10, which end with a shell that is fully restyled, fully migrated, and has an eleven-button strip) and **surfaces** (Tasks 11 to 21, which add the subsystems and redraw every panel). The first half is a complete, shippable, testable change on its own and the second half depends on it wholesale, which is the test the writing-plans skill applies.

Two arguments against, and they are why this is delivered as one plan: the companion's section 6 is one ordered list and splitting it invites the two halves to be scheduled apart, which is precisely how the icon count and the merges came to be at risk in the first place; and Task 21's completeness rule can only land at the very end, so the first plan would ship a library with no mechanical defence, which is the failure mode this whole entry exists to prevent. **The split is the orchestrator's call. If it is made, the boundary is after Task 10 and Task 21 belongs to the second plan.**

### Two things nothing in this plan tests, deliberately

- **`RunStatus.attached` stays hardcoded `false`** in four places (ruling R21). The banner's "Claude is attached" mark has never rendered, and the co-pilot bar takes its pairing truth from the pairing store instead. Fixing the plumbing is SP4c's, not this entry's.
- **There is no Claude message channel** (ruling R11). The `claude` event chip is fed by exactly three producers: `agentTokens`, the gateway health poll, and `run_started.startedBy === 'claude'`. Do not build a fourth.

## Reconciled against HEAD on 2026-09-07

SP4b landed on `sprint/dragon-slayer` and this plan was re-read against the tree at `9608f2b`. Every file the twelve `Reconcile against HEAD before dispatch` lines named was opened, every line number and symbol they cited was re-derived, and those lines were removed. This section is the record: what moved, what was added, and the five things the plan asserted that turned out to be false. Read it before dispatching a task; where it and a task body disagree, the task body wins, because the task bodies were edited to match.

### 1. What SP4b changed under this plan

Five files carry nearly all of it.

- **`web/src/frame/runBanner.ts`, 160 lines to 302.** Task 10 of SP4b added a second row (`detailEl`, `detailParts`, `paintDetail`), its collapse toggle, a health pip, and the `has-detail` root class that repositions the canvas pills. Task 14 here deletes the file.
- **`web/src/plugins/builtin/tasks.ts`, to 399 lines**, one under the 400-line ceiling: `openTraceFor` became `openReportFor`, `renderRunReport` mounts into a new `reportHost`, and `notes` (the last five health checks) is kept in the panel closure.
- **`web/src/plugins/builtin/tasksViews.ts`, to 299 lines**: `renderRunCard` and `updateRunCard` each gained a fourth positional parameter, `notes`; the run card gained four detail hooks; `renderScriptRow` gained the per-script toggle; `renderHistoryRow`'s button became `Open report`.
- **`web/src/plugins/builtin/runReportView.ts`, new**, 172 lines, with `renderTraceView`'s second mount behind its `Show trace` toggle.
- **`web/src/styles/tasks.css`, 108 lines to 174**: the `.report*` block, the run card's detail and health rules, `.task-row-toggle`, and the banner's second-row rules with four new pill offsets.

`web/src/frame/stage.ts`, `sessions/wire.ts`, `sessions/manager.ts`, `panels/connect.ts`, `frame/overlays.ts`, `main.ts` and `web/e2e/tasks.pw.test.ts` were on the "moving" list and did **not** move; their citations were confirmed rather than corrected.

### 3. Five things the plan asserted that were false

Each of these would have failed at implementation time rather than at review time, which is why they are listed apart.

1. **`data-line` does not exist** (Task 16 Step 4). It does, at `traceView.ts:63`, added by SP4b Task 11 so the copy button could join the visible lines.
2. **`setStatus(text, tone: 'muted' | 'ok' | 'warn')`** (Task 13 Interfaces). The module declares `'ok' | 'muted' | 'error'` and both callers pass `'error'`.
3. **`createMarketplaceView(deps)`** (Task 10 Interfaces). The closure it lifts calls `ctx.notify` six times, so the signature needs `ctx`.
4. **`renderRunCard(status, handlers, now)`** (Task 15 Interfaces). It has a fourth parameter, `notes`, and dropping it would have deleted SP4b Task 10's health-check list from the run card in a task about restyling.
5. **"the relative order, trace above bank, is what the mock is expressing"** (Task 7). It was not expressed by anything: `.bank-host` is at 49 and the plan put both windows near 6.

Two smaller ones, recorded for completeness: `tasks.pw.test.ts:181` asserts the bar hides, which the v2 bar never does; and `web/src/styleguide.ts` imports the module Task 14 deletes, in a file the plan named only as "a section per family".

### 4. Rulings this pass recorded

- **D88**, appended to `docs/superpowers/decisions.md`: the menu family ships as classes and a builder in Task 7; the interaction and mute go to sprint 3 entry 10, which is marked "delivered in part by Dragon Slayer entry 4".
- **D89**: the four code maps promoted to `docs/superpowers/maps/2026-09-07-shell-v2/`, closing the one item D65 left open.

Both are cross-entry, which is why they are in `decisions.md` rather than in a sub-project ledger. The remaining doors this pass applied (D22's window table, D26 and D48's z-scale, the config panel's Sound section, the event shape, the co-pilot rate line) are already ruled and needed an owner rather than a new ruling; the table above says which task took each one.
